\displaystyle\sum_{n_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\delta\frac{2m-1}{2m}n_{1}}\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{4}\right)\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{2}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{2}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}\left(\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\sum_{F_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{1}\right)}\left(F_{2}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{1}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{1}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}\left(2^{-\delta n_{2}}\left|F_{3}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{1-\frac{2}{2m}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\delta\left(1-\frac{1}{2m}\right)n_{1}-\delta\left(1-\frac{2}{2m}\right)n_{2}}\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{4}\right)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{3}\right)\left|F_{3}\right|_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2m}}\left(\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{2}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{2}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\sum_{F_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{1}\right)}\left(F_{2}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{1}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{1}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}\left|F_{3}\right|_{\mu}^{1-\frac{3}{2m}}

and continuing with
\frac{2m-4}{2m}=0
for
m=2
, we have the upper bound,

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\delta\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{2m}\right)n_{1}+\left(1-\frac{2}{2m}\right)n_{2}+\left(1-\frac{3}{2m}\right)n_{3}\right]}\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{4}\right)\left|F_{4}\right|_{\mu}^{\frac{1}{2m}}\left(\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{3}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{3}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{2}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{2}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\sum_{F_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{1}\right)}\left(F_{2}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{1}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{1}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}\left|F_{4}\right|_{\mu}^{\frac{2m-4}{2m}},

which is at most

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\delta\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{2m}\right)n_{1}+\left(1-\frac{2}{2m}\right)n_{2}+\left(1-\frac{3}{2m}\right)n_{3}\right]}\left(\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{4}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{4}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{3}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{3}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}\left(\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{2}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{2}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\sum_{F_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{1}\right)}\left(F_{2}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{1}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{1}\right|_{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}.

Finally, since

\displaystyle\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\sum_{F_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{1}\right)}\left(F_{2}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{1}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{1}\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{2m}\left|F\right|_{\mu},

\displaystyle\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\sum_{F_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{2}\right)}\left(F_{3}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{2}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{2}\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{2m}\left|F\right|_{\mu},

\displaystyle\sum_{F_{4}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{F_{3}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(n_{3}\right)}\left(F_{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F_{3}\right)^{2m}\left|F_{3}\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{2m}\left|F\right|_{\mu},

we obtain that
\mathop{\mathrm{I}nt}\left(2\right)
is dominated by

\sum_{n_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\delta\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{2m}\right)n_{1}+\left(1-\frac{2}{2m}\right)n_{2}+\left(1-\frac{3}{2m}\right)n_{3}\right]}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{2m}\left|F\right|_{\mu}=C_{\delta,p}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{2m}\left|F\right|_{\mu}\ .

This together with (
3.11
), proves

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}^{\kappa}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{4}d\mu\left(x\right)\lesssim 2^{-\delta\kappa}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{4}\left|F\right|_{\mu}\ .

Similarly, we can show for
m\geq 3
that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}^{\kappa}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2m}d\mu\left(x\right)\lesssim 2^{-\delta\kappa}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{2m}\left|F\right|_{\mu}.

Altogether then we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}^{\kappa}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)\lesssim 2^{-\delta\kappa}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{p}\left|F\right|_{\mu},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }p\in\left(0,2\right]\cup\left\{2m\right\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\ ,

where
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}^{\kappa}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F^{\kappa}}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
. Marcinkiewicz interpolation
[
GaRu
, Theorem
1.18 on page 480]
applied with the linear operator taking sequences of
numbers
\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\in\ell^{p}\left(\mathcal{F},\left|F\right|_{\mu}\right)
to sequences of functions
\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F^{\kappa}}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\in L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)
, now gives this inequality for
all
1<p<\infty
, and this completes the proof of (
3.10
),
which is the inequality in (
3.9
).

For the reverse inequality when
\kappa=0
and
2\leq p<\infty
, we have
with
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(x\right)=\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\left(x\right)
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{p}\left|F\right|_{\mu}.

3.5.
\mathcal{F}
-square functions

Recall that the Haar square function

\mathcal{S}f\left(x\right)=\mathcal{S}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}aar}}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\equiv\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

is bounded on
L^{p}\left(\mu\right)
for any
1<p<\infty
and any
locally finite positive Borel measure
\mu
by Burkholder’s theorem
[
Bur1
]
,
[
Bur2
]
and Khintchine’s inequality - see also the excellent
lecture notes
[
Hyt2
, Exercise 4 on page 19]
- simply because
\mathcal{S}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}aar}}^{\mu}
is a martingale difference square function.

We now recall extensions of this result to more complicated corona square
functions with locally finite positive Borel measures on
\mathbb{R}
that
were derived in
[
SaWi
]
(and treated there in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
). Fix a
\mathcal{D}
-dyadic interval
F_{0}
, let
\mu
be a locally finite
positive Borel measure on
F_{0}
, and suppose that
\mathcal{F}
is a
subset of
\mathcal{D}\left[F_{0}\right]\equiv\left\{I\in\mathcal{D}:I\subset F_{0}\right\}
. The collection
\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
of subsets
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\subset\mathcal{D}\left[F_{0}\right]
is
defined by

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\equiv\left\{I\in\mathcal{D}:I\subset F\text{ and }I\not\subset F^{\prime}\text{ for any }\mathcal{F}\text{-child }F^{\prime}\text{ of }F\right\},\ \ \ \ \ F\in\mathcal{F},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\text{ is connected for each }F\in\mathcal{F},

\displaystyle F\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\text{ and }I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\Longrightarrow I\subset F\text{ for each }F\in\mathcal{F},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F^{\prime}\right)=\emptyset\text{ for all distinct }F,F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\left[F_{0}\right]=\bigcup_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right).

The subset
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
of
\mathcal{D}
is
referred to as the
\mathcal{F}
-corona with top
F
, and the decomposition
\mathcal{D}\left[F_{0}\right]=\bigcup_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
is referred to as the corresponding corona
decomposition. We emphasize that there is no assumption of
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
intervals here.

Define the corona projections
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\mu}\equiv\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}
and group them together according to their
depth in the tree
\mathcal{F}
into the projections

\mathsf{P}_{k}^{\mu}\equiv\sum_{F\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{k}\left(F_{0}\right)}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\mu}\ .

Note that the
k^{th}
grandchildren
F\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{k}\left(F_{0}\right)
are pairwise disjoint and hence so are the
supports of the functions
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\mu}f
for
F\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{k}\left(F_{0}\right)
. Define the
\mathcal{F}
-square function
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{F}}f
by

\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{F}}f\left(x\right)=\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\mathsf{P}_{k}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Theorem 18

(
[
SaWi
]
)
.

Suppose
\mu
is a locally finite positive Borel measure
on
\mathbb{R}
, and let
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}
7
7

7

It was assumed in
[
SaWi
]
that
\mathcal{F}
is
\mu
-Carleson, but
this was a misprint.
. Then for
1<p<\infty
,

\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{F}}f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}\approx\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}.

Another square function that will arise in related forms is

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\rho,\delta}f\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}\ :x\in I}\left|\mathsf{P}_{I}^{\rho,\delta}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\text{where }\mathsf{P}_{I}^{\rho,\delta}f\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}:\ 2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)}2^{-\delta\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\mu}f\left(x\right).

Theorem 19
.

Suppose
\mu
is a locally finite positive Borel
measure on
\mathbb{R}
, and let
0<\rho,\delta<1
. Then for
1<p<\infty
,

\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\rho,\delta}f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}\leq C_{p,\rho,\delta}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}.

Proof.

It is easy to see that
\mathcal{S}_{\rho,\delta}f\left(x\right)\leq C_{\rho,\delta}\mathcal{S}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}aar}}f\left(x\right)
, and the
boundedness of
\mathcal{S}_{\rho,\delta}
now follows from the
boundedness of the Haar square function
\mathcal{S}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}aar}}
.

More generally, for
\Lambda\subset\mathcal{D}\left[I\right]
we define
projections

\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\equiv\sum_{J\in\Lambda}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right),

and their associated ‘absolute’ projections

(3.12)

\left|\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\omega}\right|g\left(x\right)\equiv\sqrt{\sum_{J\in\Lambda}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}.

3.5.1.
Corona martingales

The special type of martingale
\left\{f_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
that
we will be working with in this paper is that for which there is

(1)

an interval
F_{0}
(thought of as the universe) and a subset
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}\left[F_{0}\right]
,

(2)

an increasing sequence
\left\{\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
of
\sigma
-algebras of the form

\mathcal{E}_{k}\equiv\left\{E\text{ Borel }\subset F_{0}:E\cap F\in\left\{\emptyset,F\right\}\text{ for all }F\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F_{0}\right)\right\},

(3)

and a function
f\in L^{p}\left(\mu\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
such that

f_{k}\left(x\right)=\mathsf{E}_{k}^{\mu}f\left(x\right),\ \ \ \ \ x\in\mathbb{R},

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{k}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}E_{F}^{\mu}f&\text{ if }&x\in F\text{ for some }F\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F_{0}\right)\\
f\left(x\right)&\text{ if }&x\in F_{0}\setminus\bigcup\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F_{0}\right)\end{array}\right.;

\displaystyle\text{and where }\bigcup\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F_{0}\right)\equiv\bigcup_{F\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F_{0}\right)}F.

Note that the sequence
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{k}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
of corona projections of the function
f
is
the
martingale difference sequence
of the
L^{p}
bounded martingale
\left\{\mathsf{E}_{k}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}

with respect to the increasing sequence
\left\{\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
of
\sigma
-algebras generated by the ‘atoms’
F\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F_{0}\right)
.

Definition 20
.

We refer to the above construction of a martingale
difference sequence associated with the function
f
, as the
\mathcal{F}
-corona martingale difference sequence of
f
.

Conclusion 21
.

To any function
f\in L^{p}\left(\mu\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
and any subset
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}\left[F_{0}\right]
, we can associate an
L^{p}
bounded corona martingale

\left\{\mathsf{E}_{k}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
, whose martingale properties can then be exploited - e.g. Burkholder’s
theorem which leads to boundedness of the associated square function, and
the Corona Martingale Comparison Principle in Propositon
34
below.

3.5.2.
Iterated corona martingales

Given stopping times
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{A}
in a finite set, we
can view the corona decomposition associated with
\mathcal{A}
as an
iterated
corona decomposition associated with
\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}
, where the iterated stopping time
\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}
is thought
of as the union of the restricted stopping times
\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\equiv\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)
for each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
. The reason for taking this point of view is that the
corona decomposition of each corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)
into coronas
\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)\right\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}
may carry
special information that is less visible when we view the corona
decomposition
\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)\right\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}}
abstractly. In fact the usual martingale difference
sequence
\left\{h_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
associated with a function

h
and the stopping times
\mathcal{A}
, blurs any such information, since
the coronas at a given level in
\mathcal{A}
may be associated with
Q
’s
at many different levels in
\mathcal{Q}
. We will now define the
iterated
martingale difference sequence associated with
\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}
which doesn’t suffer from this defect, and clearly displays any
information peculiar to the coronas
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)
.

Define the
depth
of a stopping time
\mathcal{S}
to be the length
\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{S}\right)
of the longest tower in
\mathcal{S}
. Then in an iterated corona
\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}
we define the

iterated
difference sequence starting with the ground level of
\mathcal{Q}
, which we assume is the single interval
T
. Define
h_{1}
to
be the difference sequence associated with the sequence of coronas

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[T\right]}^{\left(1\right)}\left(T\right)=\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)\right\}_{A\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(T\right)}

which are the
\mathcal{A}\left[T\right]
children of
T
. Then set
h_{k}

to be the difference sequence associated with the coronas
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[T\right]}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)
for
1\leq k\leq D_{1}
where

D_{1}\equiv\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{A}\left[T\right]\right).

Continuing in this way beyond this point would only add vanishing difference
sequences, and corresponding repeated
\sigma
-algebras for each vanishing
difference.

Next define
h_{D_{1}+1}
to be the difference sequence associated with the
sequence of coronas
\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(1\right)}\left(Q\right)\right\}_{Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(T\right)}
at level one in
\mathcal{Q}
, and set
h_{D_{1}+k}
to
be the difference sequence associated with the sequence of coronas
\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)\right\}_{Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(T\right)}
for
1\leq k\leq D_{2}
where

D_{2}\equiv\max_{Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(T\right)}\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\right).

At this point we have defined the iterated difference sequence
\left\{h_{\ell}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{D_{1}+D_{2}}
up to
D_{1}+D_{2}
, and we now
also define the
iterated
distance
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
from the root
T
to an interval
A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]
for some
Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left[1\right]}\left(T\right)=\left\{T\right\}\cup\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(T\right)
, by

\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{A}\left[T\right]}\left(A,T\right)&\text{ if }&A\in\mathcal{A}\left[T\right]\\
D_{1}+\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A,Q\right)&\text{ if }&A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\end{array}\right.,\ \ \ \ \ A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left[1\right]}\left(T\right).

We also denote
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
by
\mathop{\mathrm{x}dist}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
when
\mathcal{Q}
is understood. Note that this iterated distance is in general
larger than the corona distance
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
.

Then we continue by defining
h_{D_{1}+D_{2}+k}
to be the difference
sequence associated with the sequence of coronas
\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)\right\}_{Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left(2\right)}\left(T\right)}
for
1\leq k\leq D_{3}
where

D_{3}\equiv\max_{Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left(2\right)}\left(T\right)}\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\right).

We also define
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
for
A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left[2\right]}\left(T\right)
in the analogous way.

We then continue this process of defining

(3.14)

h_{k},\ \ \ D_{k},\ \ \ \text{and }\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{x}dist}{}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)\text{ for }A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\text{ with }Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left[k-1\right]}\left(T\right),

until
k
has reached
\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)=D_{1}+D_{2}+...+D_{N}
, where
N=\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)
is the depth of the stopping times
\mathcal{Q}
, i.e.
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left(N\right)}\left(T\right)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left(N+1\right)}\left(T\right)=\emptyset
.

We refer to this construction of the
iterated
martingale difference
sequence
\left\{h_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)}
associated with the function
h
, as the
regularization
of the
\mathcal{A}
-corona martingale difference sequence
\left\{f_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}
,
defined in Definition
20
, by the iterated stopping times
\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}
. Note that
\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is typically much smaller than
\mathop{\mathrm{d}epth}\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)
.

Finally, we associate to each
A\in\mathcal{A}
, an ordered pair
\left(d_{1},d_{2}\right)
where
d_{1}=\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,Q\right)
and
d_{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q,T\right)

where
Q
is the unique interval in
\mathcal{Q}
such that
A\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)
. Note that the ordered pairs associated to
intervals
A
at a fixed level
\mathop{\mathrm{x}dist}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
all coincide. If we let
t
denote the level in the iterated
martingale difference sequence, then we can unambiguously define

\left(d_{1}\left(t\right),d_{2}\left(t\right)\right)\text{ to be
associated to }A\text{ where }t=\mathop{\mathrm{x}dist}{}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right).

Conclusion 22
.

Suppose we are presented with a martingale difference
sequence
\left\{f_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
for
f=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}f_{k}
relative to a collection of stopping times
\mathcal{A}
as in
Definition
20
. In the special case when
\mathcal{A}
has an
iterated structure arising from stopping times
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{A}
, we can also write
f=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}h_{\ell}
, where the
iterated martingale difference sequence
\left\{h_{\ell}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
is
finer
than
\left\{h_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}

and has a
regularizing
property, i.e. there is a function
\ell\rightarrow k=k\left(\ell\right)\leq\ell
mapping
\mathbb{N}
to
\mathbb{N}
, such that each interval
A\in\mathcal{A}
associated with a
projection
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}

occurring in the function
f_{\ell}
, is contained an interval
Q\in\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{A}
associated with a projection
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(Q\right)}
occurring in the function
h_{k\left(\ell\right)}
. Moreover, there is an iterated distance
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
, often
denoted
\mathop{\mathrm{x}dist}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)
when the iteration
is understood, in the tree
\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}
satisfying

(3.15)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{x}dist}{}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)=D_{1}+D_{2}+...+D_{m-1}+\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A,Q\right),

\displaystyle\text{for }A

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\text{ with }Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(T\right).

There is also a pair
\left(d_{1}\left(t\right),d_{2}\left(t\right)\right)
such that

\displaystyle d_{1}\left(t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,Q\right)\text{ and }d_{2}\left(t\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}{}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q,T\right),

\displaystyle\text{for all }A

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\text{ with }t=\mathop{\mathrm{x}dist}\ _{\mathcal{A}}\left(A,T\right)\text{.}

3.6.
Vector-valued inequalities

For any locally finite positive Borel measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}
, let
M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}
denote the dyadic maximal function,

M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}f\left(x\right)\equiv\sup_{x\in I\in\mathcal{D}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}\int_{I}\left|f\right|d\mu\right)\mathbf{1}_{I}\left(x\right),

which is well-known to be bounded on
L^{p}\left(\mu\right)
for
1<p\leq\infty
(since the weak type
\left(1,1\right)
and strong type
\left(\infty,\infty\right)
constants are both
1
). We need the
\ell^{2}

vector-valued inequality of Fefferman and Stein for the dyadic maximal
operator
M_{\mu}
for
1<p<\infty
, namely

(3.16)

\left\|\left|M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\mathbf{f}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}\lesssim\left\|\left|\mathbf{f}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)},\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<\infty,

where
\mathbf{f}\left(x\right)=\left\{f_{i}\left(x\right)\right\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
,
M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\mathbf{f}=\left\{M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}f_{i}\left(x\right)\right\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
and
\left|\mathbf{h}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left|h_{i}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}
. We are unable to find
this statement explicitly in the literature for general measures, and we
thank José-Luis Luna-Garcia for pointing out to us that the case
p\geq 2
follows from the duality argument in
[
FeSt
]
, and that the case
1<p\leq 2
then follows from the weak type
\left(1,1\right)
inequality
in
[
CrMaPe
, Theorem A.15 on page 247]
, together with Marcinkiewicz
interpolation for Banach space valued functions, see e.g.
[
GaRu
, Theorem 1.18
on page 480]
. For the convenience of the reader, we repeat the short arguments suggested by José here.

Proof of (
3.16
) (J.-L. Luna-Garcia).

First, for any weight
w
, we claim that

(3.17)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}f\left(x\right)\right|^{q}w\left(x\right)d\mu\left(x\right)\leq C_{q}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|f\left(x\right)\right|^{q}M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}w\left(x\right)d\mu\left(x\right),\ \ \ \ \ 1<q<\infty.

Indeed, let
\lambda>0
and suppose
\Omega_{\lambda}\equiv\left\{M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}f>\lambda\right\}=\overset{\cdot}{\bigcup}_{j=1}^{\infty}I_{j}
where
I_{j}
are the maximal dyadic intervals
satisfying
\frac{1}{\left|I_{j}\right|_{\mu}}\int_{I_{j}}\left|f\right|d\mu>\lambda
. Then we have the weak type
\left(1,1\right)
inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\left\{M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}f>\lambda\right\}\right|_{w\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\left|I_{j}\right|_{w\mu}=\sum_{j}\left(\frac{1}{\left|I_{j}\right|_{\mu}}\left|I_{j}\right|_{w\mu}\right)\left|I_{j}\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\left(\frac{1}{\left|I_{j}\right|_{\mu}}\left|I_{j}\right|_{w\mu}\right)\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{I_{j}}\left|f\right|d\mu\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|f\right|\left(M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}w\right)d\mu,

as well as the strong type
\left(\infty,\infty\right)
inequality.
Marcinkiewicz interpolation now gives (
3.17
).

From (
3.17
) with
q=\frac{p}{2}\geq 1
we have,

\displaystyle\left\|\left|M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\mathbf{f}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left|M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}f_{i}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\mu\right)^{\frac{2}{p}}=\sup_{\left\|g\right\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}\left(\sigma\right)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left|M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}f_{i}\right|^{2}\right)gd\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\|g\right\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}\left(\mu\right)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left|f_{i}\right|^{2}M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}gd\mu\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left|f_{i}\right|^{2}\right)^{q}d\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}g\right)^{q^{\prime}}d\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{q}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left|f_{i}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\mu\right)^{\frac{2}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|g\right|^{q^{\prime}}d\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}=C_{q}\left\|\left|\mathbf{f}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ .

This completes the proof that (
3.16
) holds for
p\geq 2
.

The weak type
\left(1,1\right)
inequality in
[
CrMaPe
, Theorem A.15 on page
247]
says that

\left|\left\{\left|M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\mathbf{f}\right|_{\ell^{2}}>\lambda\right\}\right|_{\mu}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}\int\left|\mathbf{f}\right|_{\ell^{2}}d\mu,

and now the Marcinkiewicz interpolation theorem in
[
GaRu
, Theorem 1.18 on
page 480]
completes the proof of (
3.16
).

Projections and maximal operators:

We will sometimes apply (
3.16
) in conjunction with the fact that, by the telescoping identity for Haar
projections
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{D}}
, a projection
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}
with
\Lambda
a connected
subset of
\mathcal{D}\left[S\right]\setminus\left\{S\right\}
for some

S\in\mathcal{D}
, is dominated pointwise by the dyadic maximal operator,

(3.18)

\left|\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right|\leq 2M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{S}f\right)\left(x\right).

Indeed, for any
x
, if
I
is the smallest interval in
\Lambda

containing
x
, and if
F\in\Lambda
is the largest, then

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)=\sum_{K\in\left[I,S_{I}\right)}\left(E_{K}^{\mu}f-E_{\pi K}^{\mu}f\right)=E_{I}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)-E_{\pi F}^{\mu}f\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\text{and }\left|\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right|\leq 2M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{S}f\right)\left(x\right)\text{ \ since }\pi F\subset S,

where
\pi K
is the parent of
K
in the dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
. Since
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}f=\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}f\right)
we also have

(3.19)

\left|\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\right|\leq 2M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\mu}f\right)\left(x\right).

We will also need the following ‘disjoint support’ lemma for vector-valued
functions. Define the mixed norm space

L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)\equiv\left\{\mathbf{f}=\left(f_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{\infty}:\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}\equiv\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbf{f}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}<\infty\right\}.

Lemma 23
.

Let
1<p<\infty
. Suppose
\left\{f^{n}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is a sequence in
L^{p}\left(\ell^{2},\mu\right)

where
f^{n}\left(x\right)=\left\{f_{k}^{n}\left(x\right)\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\in\ell^{2}
, and that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
, the
\mathbb{N}
-supports

\mathbb{N}\text{-}\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}f^{n}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{k\in\mathbb{N}:f_{k}^{n}\left(x\right)\neq 0\right\}

of
f^{n}\left(x\right)
are pairwise disjoint in
n
, i.e.

(3.20)

\mathbb{N}\text{-}\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}f^{n}\left(x\right)\cap\mathbb{N}\text{-}\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}f^{m}\left(x\right)=\emptyset,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }n\neq m.

Then

(3.21)

\left\|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|f^{n}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\mu\left(x\right).

If the functions
f^{n}\left(x\right)
are pairwise disjoint in
x
, i.e.
the
\mathbb{R}
-supports

\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}f^{n}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}:f^{n}\left(x\right)\neq 0\right\}

of
f^{n}
satisfy

(3.22)

\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}f^{n}\cap\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}f^{m}=\emptyset,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }n\neq m,

then

(3.23)

\left\|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left\|f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}\ .

Proof.

For each
x\in\mathbb{R}
, the disjoint
\mathbb{N}
-support hypothesis on
the sequence
\left\{f^{n}\left(x\right)\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
yields

\left|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}f^{n}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|f^{n}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}
, which gives (
3.21
). If the sequence
\left\{f^{n}\left(x\right)\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is pairwise disjoint in
x
,
then for all
1<p<\infty\,.
we have

\left\|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|f^{n}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\mu\left(x\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|f^{n}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left\|f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}.

Inequality (
3.23
) will be used throughout the paper, and
especially in the proof of the Corona Martingale Comparison Principle.

Corollary 24
.

Let
1<p<\infty
. Suppose
\left\{f^{n}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
\left\{g^{n}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
are sequences
in
L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)
, each satisfying (
3.22
), and
that there is
\eta>0
such that
\left\|f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}\leq\eta\left\|g^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\left\|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}\leq\eta\left\|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}g^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}.

Proof.

From the lemma above we have

\left\|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left\|f^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}\leq\eta^{p}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left\|g^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}=\eta^{p}\left\|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}g^{n}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\mu\right)}^{p}.

We close this section with a technical lemma that is needed in connection
with the dual tree decomposition below that generalizes the upside down
corona construction of M. Lacey in
[
Lac
]
.

Lemma 25
.

Let
1<p<2
. Suppose
G\left(x\right)=\left\{G_{k}\left(x\right)\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and
B\left(x\right)=\left\{B_{k}\left(x\right)\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
are two sequences of
functions on the real line with pairwise disjoint
\mathbb{N}
-supports,

G_{k}\left(x\right)B_{k}\left(x\right)=0,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }k\text{
and }x.

Then there is a positive constant
c>0
(independent of the sequences) such
that

(3.24)

\max\left\{\frac{\Lambda^{p}}{\left\|G\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}},\left(\frac{\Lambda^{p}}{\left\|G\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right\}\geq c\frac{\left\|B\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\left\|G\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}},

where

\Lambda^{p}\equiv\left\|G+B\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}-\left\|G\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}.

Proof.

Lemma
23
, together with the pairwise disjoint
\mathbb{N}
-support hypotheisis, shows that

\displaystyle\Lambda^{p}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left|G_{k}\left(x\right)+B_{k}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left|G_{k}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left|G_{k}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left|B_{k}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left|G_{k}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|G\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}+\left|B\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|G\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right),

where
g\left(x\right)\equiv\left|G\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}
and
b\left(x\right)\equiv\left|B\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}
are the functions we work with from now on.

For
0<\eta\ll 1
, we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{\eta}+C_{\eta}+R_{\eta}\ ,

\displaystyle\text{where }L_{\eta}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{b\left(x\right)\leq\eta g\left(x\right)\right\},

\displaystyle C_{\eta}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\eta g\left(x\right)<b\left(x\right)<\frac{1}{\eta}g\left(x\right)\right\},

\displaystyle\text{and }R_{\eta}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{g\left(x\right)\leq\eta b\left(x\right)\right\},

and then decompose

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)=\left\{\int_{L_{\eta}}+\int_{C_{\eta}}+\int_{R_{\eta}}\right\}\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)\equiv T_{L_{\eta}}+T_{C_{\eta}}+T_{R_{\eta}}.

We have

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{L_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{L_{\eta}}\leq\left(1+\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{L_{\eta}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\left(1+\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{C_{\eta}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{C_{\eta}}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{C_{\eta}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{R_{\eta}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{R_{\eta}}\leq\left(1+\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{R_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right),

and so

\displaystyle\Lambda^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{L_{\eta}\cup C_{\eta}\cup R_{\eta}}\left[\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-g\left(x\right)^{p}\right]d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{L_{\eta}}\left[\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-g\left(x\right)^{p}\right]d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+\left[\left(1+\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-1\right]\int_{C_{\eta}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{R_{\eta}}\left[\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-g\left(x\right)^{p}\right]d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left[\left(1+\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-1\right]\int_{C_{\eta}}\left(\eta b\left(x\right)\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)+\left\{\int_{L_{\eta}}+\int_{R_{\eta}}\right\}\left[\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-g\left(x\right)^{p}\right]d\omega\left(x\right).

In particular,

\int_{C_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\leq\frac{\Lambda^{p}}{\left[\left(1+\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-1\right]\eta^{p}}\lesssim\eta^{-p-2}\Lambda^{p}.

Since
g\left(x\right)\leq\eta b\left(x\right)
on
R_{\eta}
, we also
have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{R_{\eta}}\left[\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-g\left(x\right)^{p}\right]d\omega\left(x\right)=\int_{R_{\eta}}\left[\left(\left(\frac{g\left(x\right)}{b\left(x\right)}\right)^{2}+1\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-\left(\frac{g\left(x\right)}{b\left(x\right)}\right)^{p}\right]b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{R_{\eta}}\left(1-\eta^{p}\right)b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)=\left(1-\eta^{p}\right)\int_{R_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right),

and so altogether we have

(3.25)

\int_{C_{\eta}\cup R_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\eta^{-p-2}\Lambda^{p}.

Now we continue differently in two exhaustive cases.

Case I:
\int_{\mathbb{R}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\leq\eta^{-1}\int_{C_{\eta}\cup R_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)
.

In this case (
3.25
) yields

\int_{\mathbb{R}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\eta^{-p-2-1}\Lambda^{p}.

Case II:
\int_{C_{\eta}\cup R_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)<\eta\int_{\mathbb{R}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)
.

Since
b\left(x\right)\leq\eta g\left(x\right)
on
L_{\eta}
, we have
for
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{p}\geq\int_{L_{\eta}}\left[\left(g\left(x\right)^{2}+b\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-g\left(x\right)^{p}\right]d\omega\left(x\right)=\int_{L_{\eta}}\left[\left(1+\left(\frac{b\left(x\right)}{g\left(x\right)}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}-1\right]g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\int_{L_{\eta}}\left(\frac{b\left(x\right)}{g\left(x\right)}\right)^{2}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)=\int_{L_{\eta}}\frac{b\left(x\right)^{2}}{g\left(x\right)^{2-p}}d\omega\left(x\right).

Recall that
1<p<2
, so that we can apply Hölder’s inequality with
exponents
\frac{2}{p}
and
\frac{2}{2-p}
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{L_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)=\int_{L_{\eta}}\frac{b\left(x\right)^{p}}{g\left(x\right)^{p\left(1-\frac{p}{2}\right)}}g\left(x\right)^{p\left(1-\frac{p}{2}\right)}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{L_{\eta}}\frac{b\left(x\right)^{2}}{g\left(x\right)^{2-p}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\int_{L_{\eta}}g\left(x\right)^{p\left(1-\frac{p}{2}\right)\frac{2}{2-p}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{L_{\eta}}\frac{b\left(x\right)^{2}}{g\left(x\right)^{2-p}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\int_{L_{\eta}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\lesssim\left(\Lambda^{p}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}.

Because we are in Case II, we also have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{L_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)+\int_{C_{\eta}\cup R_{\eta}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\Lambda^{p}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}+\eta\int_{\mathbb{R}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\leq\frac{\left(\Lambda^{p}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}}{1-\eta}.

Thus altogether we have shown that for
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}
,

(3.26)

\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}b\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)}\leq\max\left\{\eta^{-p-2-1}\frac{\Lambda^{p}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)},\frac{1}{1-\eta}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{p}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right\},

and this completes the proof of (
3.24
) upon taking
\eta=\frac{1}{2}
.

4.
Beginning the proof of the main theorems

We build our proof on the decomposition used in
[
Saw7
]
- with the
exception of the bounds for the far and stopping forms, which require new
arguments. We assume that the Haar supports of the functions
f\in L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)
and
g\in L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\omega\right)
in
the proof are contained in the
\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}
-
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
grid
\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}}
. Here is a brief schematic
diagram of the initial twelve decompositions made below,

(4.1)

\framebox{$\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}\left\langle H_{\sigma}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}&&&&&&&&&&\\
\downarrow&&&&&&&&&&\\
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}^{\ast}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\ast}\left(f,g\right)\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow&&&&&&&&\\
\downarrow&&\mathop{\mathrm{s}imilar}&&&&&&&&\\
\downarrow&&&&&&&&&&\\
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)&&&&\end{array}$}\ ,

where we note that there is a similar decomposition of
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}\left(f,g\right)
into dual neighbour, far, paraproduct and
stopping forms.

The long-range portion
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)
of the disjoint form will be controlled by the triple quadratic
Muckenhoupt characteristics, but will
also
be controlled by the
global quadratic testing characteristics. Similarly for the far form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)
. Here are all of the bounds
listed in the order we will prove them (all bounds except for (9) are valid
for
1<p<\infty
, and their duals follow by symmetry)
8
8

8

The stopping form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)
depends
only on the scalar testing characteristic
\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
through the stopping energy
\mathfrak{X}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
and the
\sigma
-Carleson condition.
:

(4.2)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(1)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{WBP}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(2)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(3)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(4)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{m}id}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(5)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(6)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{A}_{p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{p}unct}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{E}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(7)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{E}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(8)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}\ ,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{(9)}

\displaystyle\ \left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)},\ 1<p<4\ .

These bounds, together with the necessity results above, complete the proofs
of both Theorems
5
and
7
(the dual of
\left(9\right)
requires
1<p^{\prime}<4
as well) because the global quadratic
testing characteristic dominates all the other characteristics, with the
exception of the triple quadratic Muckenhoupt characteristic. Note that the
quadratic weak boundedness characteristic is used only for the
comparable
form, that the triple quadratic Muckenhoupt characteristic is
used only for the long-range portion of the
disjoint
form, and that
the scalar tailed Muckenhoupt characteristic is used only for the
far

form. Each of the bounds (2) - (5) and (7) - (9) involve just one of our
quadratic hypotheses on the right hand side, while in the first bound (1),
the reader can easily check that the comparable form can be naturally
decomposed into three pieces (with overlapping, separted and adjacent
intervals), each of which are bounded by just one of the characteristics,
but there are too many decompositions of the functional energy inequality to
separate out where each characteristic is being used for control of the far
form.

We now describe these decompositions in detail, repeating what is needed
from
[
Saw7
]
for the convenience of the reader. Following
[
NTV4
]

and
[
LaSaShUr3
]
, we fix a dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
and assume without
loss of generality, see e.g.
[
NTV4
, (4.3) in Section 4]
, that both
f

and
g
are supported in a fixed dyadic interval
T\in\mathcal{D}
and have

\int_{T}fd\sigma=\int_{T}gd\omega=0
. We first expand the Hilbert
transform bilinear form
\left\langle H_{\sigma}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}

in terms of the Haar decompositions of
f
and
g
,

\left\langle H_{\sigma}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{I,J\in\mathcal{D}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega},

and then assuming the Haar supports of
f
and
g
lie in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}}
, we decompose the double sum above as
follows,

\displaystyle\left\langle H_{\sigma}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
I\subset_{\tau}J\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
I\cap J=\emptyset\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)<2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
I\cap J=\emptyset\text{ and }\ell\left(I\right)<2^{-\tau}\ell\left(J\right)\end{subarray}}\right.

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left.\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
I\subset J\text{ and }\ell\left(I\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(J\right)\end{subarray}}\right\}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}^{\ast}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\ast}\left(f,g\right),

where

\tau=r+1,

and where
J\subset_{\tau}I
is defined in (
3.2
). The
first two forms are symmetric, and so it suffices to prove the boundedness
of just one of them, say
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)
, for all
1<p<\infty
. Indeed, with the more precise definitions

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}^{H,\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\ \text{and }\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}^{H,\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
I\subset_{\tau}J\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\ ,

we have

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}^{H,\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J,I\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\omega}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\omega}g,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\sigma}f\right\rangle_{\sigma}=-\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}^{H,\left(\omega,\sigma\right)}\left(g,f\right).

Using a Calderón-Zygmund corona decomposition with parameter
\Gamma>1
, we will later decompose the below form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)
into another four forms,

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)=\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right),

in which there is control of averages of
f
in each corona. At this point
we will have twelve forms in our decomposition of the inner product
\left\langle H_{\sigma}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}
.

4.1.
Comparable form

We will bound the comparable form

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left\langle\mathbf{1}_{J}H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

for
1<p<\infty
, by the local quadratic testing, offset quadratic
Muckenhoupt, and quadratic weak boundedness characteristics, i.e. we prove

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{WBP}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ .

This is the only place in this paper where we use the quadratic weak
boundedness characteristic
\mathcal{WBP}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
. Note also that the second inequality has already been
proved in the section on necessity.

We write

\displaystyle\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}+\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}\ ,

\displaystyle\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(E_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}+\left(E_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left(E_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\left\langle\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}},\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left(E_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\left\langle\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}},\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left(E_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\left\langle\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}},\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left(E_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\left\langle\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}},\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft},\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft},\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight},\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight},\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}\left(f,g\right).

If the pair of intervals
I_{\zeta/\eta}
and
J_{\zeta/\eta}
are
disjoint,
\zeta,\eta\in\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft},\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}\right\}
,
then the sums above are immediately controlled by the quadratic weak
boundedness characteristic when they are adjacent, and by the quadratic
offset Muckenhoupt characteristic when they are not.

If the intervals overlap, then using the
\ell^{2}
Cauchy-Schwarz and
L^{p}\left(\omega\right)
Hölder inequalities we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq\sum_{\zeta,\eta\in\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft},\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}\right\}}\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(f,g\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\zeta,\eta\in\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft},\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}\right\}}\left\|\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{,
}\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\text{ and }I_{\zeta}\cap J_{\eta}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left|\left(E_{I_{\zeta}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\mathbf{1}_{J_{\eta}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\zeta}}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\left\|\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\left(E_{J_{\eta}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\mathbf{1}_{J_{\eta}}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

The second factor is dominated by

\left\|\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\subset I\text{
and }\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\left(\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right)\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{S}^{\omega}g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\approx\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}

by the square function estimate in Theorem
18
, and since there
are only
\tau+1
intervals
I\in\mathcal{D}
with
J\subset I
and
\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)
.

We now turn to the first factor on the right side above where the intervals
I_{\zeta}
and
J_{\eta}
overlap, and consider separately the cases
J=I

and
J_{\eta}\subset I_{\zeta}
for some choice of
\zeta,\eta\in\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft},\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}\right\}
. For the case
J=I
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)^{2}\left|\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)},

and similarly

\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left(E_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)^{2}\left|\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\ .

For those
J_{\eta}\subset I_{\zeta}
, we apply the local quadratic
testing condition to
H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\zeta}}
, and again finish
with the square function Theorem
18
. Finally, the estimate for
the dual comparable form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}^{\ast}\left(f,g\right)
is handled symmetrically.

4.2.
Disjoint form

Here we prove the following estimates for the disjoint form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}\left(f,g\right)
with absolute values inside the sum:

(4.3)

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}\right|\left(f,g\right)\equiv\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\cap I=\emptyset\text{ and
}\ell\left(J\right)<2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<\infty,

and

(4.4)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}\right|\left(f,g\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<\infty.

This is the only place in this paper where we use the triple quadratic
Muckenhoupt characteristic
A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
, or make
direct
use of the global quadratic testing
characteristic
\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
.

Proof of (
4.3
) and (
4.4
).

We further decompose the form
\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}\right|\left(f,g\right)
as

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isj}}\right|\left(f,g\right)\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
I\cap J=\emptyset\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}+\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{m}id}}\left(f,g\right).

The long-range case:
Here we prove that the long-range form
\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)
can be bounded
either
by the triple quadratic Muckenhoupt characteristic
A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
,
or
by the global
quadratic testing characteristic
\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
.

Claim #1

(4.5)

\left|\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|\lesssim A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

Proof
: In the sum in the middle of the display above, we pigeonhole
the intervals
I
and
J
relative to intervals
K\in\mathcal{D}
. Let
N,s,t\in\mathbb{N}
. For
K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}\equiv\left\{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ell\left(I\right)=2^{N}\right\}
, we restrict
I
and
J
to
I\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}
and
J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}
respectively and write

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s,t\in\mathbb{N}}\left\{\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}\\
I\subset K\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}\\
d\left(J,I\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|\right\}=\sum_{s,t\in\mathbb{N}}W_{s,t},

\displaystyle\text{where }W_{s,t}\equiv\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}\\
I\subset K\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}\\
d\left(J,I\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|,

and observe that

W_{s,t}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}\\
d\left(J,I\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}\\
I\subset K\end{subarray}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|\right\}d\omega\left(x\right).

By the Monotonicity Lemma and Poisson Decay Lemma, this is bounded by,

\displaystyle W_{s,t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}\\
d\left(J,I\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)\end{subarray}}\mathrm{P}\left(J,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}\\
I\subset K\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|\right\}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\mathrm{P}\left(K,f_{K}^{N}\sigma\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)\ g_{K}^{N}\left(x\right)\ d\omega\left(x\right),

where

\displaystyle f_{K}^{N}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}\\
I\subset K\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|\text{ and }g_{K}^{N}\left(x\right)\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}\\
d\left(J,I\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|,

\displaystyle\text{and }g_{K}^{N}\left(x\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{s+t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}\\
d\left(J,I\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle W_{s,t}\lesssim 2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)}\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\mathrm{P}\left(K,f_{K}^{N}\sigma\right)\mathbf{1}_{3K\setminus K}\left(x\right)\ g_{K}^{N}\left(x\right)\ d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\mathrm{P}\left(K,f_{K}^{N}\sigma\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{3K\setminus K}\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left|g_{K}^{N}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left(\frac{\int_{K}f_{K}^{N}d\sigma}{\left|K\right|}\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{3K\setminus K}\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}2^{\frac{s+t}{2}}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s-t}\\
d\left(J,I\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}f_{K}^{N}\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}2^{\frac{s+t}{2}}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

where we have used the triple quadratic Muckenhoupt condition in the last
line, after breaking up the annulus
3K\setminus K
into its left and right
hand intervals, and where we have also used
\left\|\mathcal{S}g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\approx\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}
.

Moreover, this last line is dominated by

\displaystyle W_{s,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon\right)}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}\\
I\subset K\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon\right)}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

this time using
\left\|\mathcal{S}f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\approx\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}
.
Finally we sum in
s
and
t
to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{s,t\in\mathbb{N}}W_{s,t}\lesssim\left(\sum_{s,t\in\mathbb{N}}2^{-\left(s+t\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon\right)}\right)A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},

provided we take
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{4}
.

Claim #2

(4.6)

\left|\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{G}:\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

Proof
: Let
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and restricting
I
and
J
to
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)
and
\ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)
, we write

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}W_{t}\ ,

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \text{where }W_{t}\equiv\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|.

Now

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{+,-}\left(E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega,

\displaystyle\text{where }\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega,

\displaystyle\text{and }\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}},h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega},

\displaystyle\text{and }\left|\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}},h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{J}\int_{I_{\pm}}\left\{\frac{1}{y-z}-\frac{1}{y-c_{J}}\right\}d\sigma\left(y\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(z\right)d\omega\left(z\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{J}\int_{I_{\pm}}\frac{\left(z-c_{J}\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(z\right)}{\left(y-z\right)\left(y-c_{J}\right)}d\sigma\left(y\right)d\omega\left(z\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{J}\int_{I_{\pm}}\left|\frac{\left(z-c_{J}\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(z\right)}{\left(y-z\right)\left(y-c_{J}\right)}\right|d\sigma\left(y\right)d\omega\left(z\right),

since

h_{J}^{\omega}=\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{+}}-\frac{1}{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{-}}\right),

implies that neither
\left(z-c_{J}\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(z\right)

nor
\left(y-z\right)\left(y-c_{J}\right)
changes sign in the region of
integration. Thus

\left|\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}},h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\approx\left(\int_{I_{\pm}}\frac{1}{\left(y-c_{J}\right)^{2}}d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)\left|\int_{J}\left(z-c_{J}\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(z\right)d\omega\left(z\right)\right|

and

\displaystyle\int_{J}\left(z-c_{J}\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(z\right)d\omega\left(z\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{J}\left(z-c_{J}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{+}}-\frac{1}{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{-}}\right)d\omega\left(z\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}\int_{J_{+}}\left(z-c_{J}\right)d\omega\left(z\right)-\frac{1}{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}}\int_{J_{-}}\left(z-c_{J}\right)d\omega\left(z\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}\left(m_{J_{+}}-m_{J_{-}}\right),

where
m_{J_{+}}=w_{+}-c_{J}
and
m_{J_{-}}=w_{-}-c_{J}
with
w_{\pm}\in J_{\pm}
. Thus

\displaystyle\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\left(x\right)\right|=\left|h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}},h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left(\int_{I_{\pm}}\frac{1}{\left(y-c_{J}\right)^{2}}d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)\left|h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)\int_{J}\left(z-c_{J}\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(z\right)d\omega\left(z\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{I_{\pm}}\frac{1}{\left(y-c_{J}\right)^{2}}d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)\left|m_{J_{+}}-m_{J_{-}}\right|\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}\left|h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{I_{\pm}}\frac{1}{\left(y-c_{J}\right)^{2}}d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)\left|m_{J_{+}}-m_{J_{-}}\right|\frac{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|J_{+}\right|_{\omega}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{+}}\left(x\right)-\frac{1}{\left|J_{-}\right|_{\omega}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{-}}\left(x\right)\right),

where
