\left|m_{J_{+}}-m_{J_{-}}\right|=\left|w_{+}-w_{-}\right|\leq\ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right).

We also have

\inf_{z\in J}H_{\sigma}I_{\pm}\left(z\right)=\inf_{z\in J}\int_{I_{\pm}}\frac{1}{y-z}d\sigma\left(y\right)\geq\frac{\left|I_{\pm}\right|_{\sigma}}{2\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)},

and so altogether we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\left(x\right)\right|\leq\left(\int_{I_{\pm}}\frac{1}{\left(y-c_{J}\right)^{2}}d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)^{2}}\left|I_{\pm}\right|_{\sigma}2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)\leq 2^{2-t}\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)}H_{\sigma}I_{\pm}\left(x\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right).

We conclude that for each fixed
I\in\mathcal{D}
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{+,-}\left(E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\right)\left(x\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{2-t}\sum_{+,-}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\left|E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|\left|H_{\sigma}I_{\pm}\left(x\right)\right|\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)\right)\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|\right)d\omega\left(x\right)

and so

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{2-t}\sum_{+,-}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\left|H_{\sigma}I_{\pm}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{2-t}\sum_{+,-}\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\left|H_{\sigma}I_{\pm}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},

where the first norm satisifies

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\left|H_{\sigma}I_{\pm}\right|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{J}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\left|H_{\sigma}I_{\pm}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|E_{\pm}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\leq\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)},

(note that we choose either
+
throughout or
-
throughout) and the second
norm satisfies

\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{-t}\ell\left(I\right)\\
d\left(J,I\right)>\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right|^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\left\|\sqrt{\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right|^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

The square function inequalities in Theorem
18
were used in
both estimates above.

The mid range case
: Here we prove that the mid-range form
\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{m}id}}\left(f,g\right)
can be bounded by the quadratic offset
Muckenhoupt characteristic
A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}
.

Let

\mathcal{P}\equiv\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}:J\text{ is good},\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{-\tau}\ell\left(I\right),\text{ }J\subset 3I\setminus I\right\}.

Now we pigeonhole the lengths of
I
and
J
and the distance between them
by defining

\mathcal{P}_{N,m}^{t}\equiv\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}:J\text{ is }\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood},\ \ell\left(I\right)=2^{N},\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{N-t},\text{ }J\subset 3I\setminus I,\ 2^{N-m-1}\leq\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\leq 2^{N-m}\right\}.

Note that the closest a good cube
J
can come to
I
is determined by the
goodness inequality, which gives this bound for
2^{N-m}\geq\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)
:

\displaystyle 2^{N-m}\geq\frac{1}{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{1-\varepsilon}\ell\left(J\right)^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}2^{N\left(1-\varepsilon\right)}2^{\left(N-t\right)\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}2^{N-\varepsilon t};

\displaystyle\text{which implies }0\leq m\leq\varepsilon t,

where the last inequality holds because we are in the case of the mid-range
term.

Now we use
\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}\equiv\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,m}^{t}\text{\ }}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}
, and apply Cauchy-Schwarz in
I
with
J\subset 3I\setminus I
to get

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,m}^{t}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f,\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\ \mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left|\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left|\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left|\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left|\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},

where the second factor is at most
C\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}
by the pairwise disjoint Haar supports of the
projections
\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}
.

Now recall that for fixed
I
, the intervals
J
such that
\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,m}^{t}
satisfy

\ell\left(I\right)=2^{N},\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{N-t},\text{ }J\subset 3I\setminus I,\ 2^{N-m-1}\leq\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\leq 2^{N-m},

and so for
y\in I
we have
\left|y-c_{J}\right|\geq 2^{N-m}
and
so

\mathrm{P}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)=\int_{I}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left(\ell\left(J\right)+\left|y-c_{J}\right|\right)^{2}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(y\right)\right|d\sigma\left(y\right)\lesssim 2^{N-t-2\left(N-m\right)}\int_{I}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(y\right)\right|d\sigma\left(y\right),

and we obtain

\left|\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}:\ \left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,m}^{t}}\mathrm{P}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)\lesssim 2^{-t+2m}\left(\int_{I}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\left(y\right)\right|d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{3I\setminus I}\left(x\right).

Thus the first factor satisfies,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left|\mathsf{Q}_{I,t,N,m}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-t+2m}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left(\int_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|d\sigma+\int_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|d\sigma\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{3I\setminus I}\left(x\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-t+2m}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left(\frac{\left|I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\left|I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|d\sigma\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{3I\setminus I}\left(x\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle+2^{-t+2m}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left(\frac{\left|I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\left|I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|d\sigma\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{3I\setminus I}\left(x\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}.

By the quadratic offset Muckenhoupt condition, the first term on the right
hand side involving
I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}
is at most

\displaystyle A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)2^{-t+2m}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|d\sigma\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)2^{-t+2m}\left\|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}}\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\lesssim A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)2^{-t+2m}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)},

where we have used that
\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|
is constant on
I_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}eft}}
, followed by the square
function bound. Similarly for the second term on the right hand side
involving
I_{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ight}}
.

Summing in
t
and
m
we then have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{m}id}}\left(f,g\right)\right|=\left|\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,m}^{t}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\leq\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}\left|\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,m}^{t}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)2^{-t+2m}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},

since
\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon t}2^{-t+2m}\leq\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1+\varepsilon t\right)2^{-t\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)}\leq C_{\varepsilon}
if
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
.

This completes the proof of both (
4.3
) and (
4.4
).

5.
Decomposition of the below form

Let

\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\equiv\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}}\times\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}}:J\subset_{\tau}I\right\}

be the set of pairs of
\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}
-
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
dyadic intervals
\left(I,J\right)
with
J
at least
\tau
levels below and inside
I
.
We begin by splitting the
\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}
form into home and neighbour forms,
where
\theta K
denotes the dyadic sibling of
K\in\mathcal{D}
, and
I_{J}
denotes the child of
I
that contains
J
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}+\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\theta I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{h}ome}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right).

5.1.
Neighbour form

The neighbour form is controlled by the quadratic offset Muckenhoupt
condition using Lemma
11
and the fact that the intervals
J

are good, namely we claim

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)},\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<\infty.

We have

\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I_{J}\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}=E_{\theta\left(I_{J}\right)}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\cdot\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I_{J}\right)},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega},

and thus we can write

(5.1)

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{I,J\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\text{ and }J\subset_{\tau}I}\left(E_{\theta\left(I_{J}\right)}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I_{J}\right)},\Delta_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\ .

To see the claim, momentarily fix an integer
s\geq\tau
. Now we
pigeonhole pairs
\left(I,J\right)
of intervals by requiring
J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s\right)}\left(I\right)
, i.e.
J\subset I
and
\ell\left(J\right)=2^{-s}\ell\left(I\right)
, and we
further separate the two cases where
I_{J}=I_{\pm}
, the right and left
children of
I
, so that we have

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{I}\sum_{+,-}\sum_{s=\tau}^{\infty}=\sum_{+,-}\sum_{s=\tau}^{\infty}\sum_{J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s-1\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\Delta_{J}^{\omega}g\rangle_{\omega}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\left|A_{\pm}\left(I,s\right)\right|

\displaystyle\equiv\left|\sum_{J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s-1\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\Delta_{J}^{\omega}g\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{I,J\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\text{ and }J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s-1\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\left(E_{I_{\mp}}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \left\langle\Delta_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}},\Delta_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{I,J\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\text{
and }J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s-1\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\left(E_{I_{\mp}}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \Delta_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\left(x\right)\ \Delta_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|.

If we now apply
\ell^{2}
Cauchy-Schwarz followed by
L^{p}\left(\omega\right)
Hölder, we obtain

\left|A_{\pm}\left(I,s\right)\right|\leq\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}\text{, }J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s-1\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\left|\left(E_{I_{\mp}}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \Delta_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}\text{, }J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s-1\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\left|\Delta_{J}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

The second factor is at most
C\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}
by the square function inequality.

For the first factor we use the pointwise Monotonicity Lemma
13
,

\left|\Delta_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\left(x\right)\right|\leq 2\mathrm{P}\left(J,\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\sigma\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right),

and then the Poisson Decay Lemma
14
, to obtain

\left|\Delta_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\left(x\right)\right|\leq 2\mathrm{P}\left(J,\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\sigma\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)\leq C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}\mathrm{P}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\sigma\right)\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right).

Thus the first factor is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}\left\|\left(\sum_{I,J\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\text{ and }J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\left|E_{I_{\mp}}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\mathrm{P}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\sigma\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}\left|E_{I_{\mp}}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\left(\frac{\left|I_{\mp}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|}\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)},

upon using
\mathrm{P}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mp}}\sigma\right)\lesssim\frac{\left|I_{\mp}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|}

and
\sum_{J\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(s-1\right)}\left(I_{\pm}\right)}\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(x\right)=\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\left(x\right)
.

Now we conclude from the quadratic offset Muckenhoupt condition that the
above term is at most

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}\left|E_{I_{\mp}}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\left(\frac{\left|I_{\mp}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|}\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}\left|E_{I_{\mp}}^{\sigma}\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}\left|\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)},

where we have used that
\left|\Delta_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|
is
constant on each child of
I
, followed by the square function estimate.
Then we can sum in
\pm
and
s
to obtain for
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eigh}}\left(f,g\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{+,-}\sum_{s=\tau}^{\infty}\left|A_{\pm}\left(I,s\right)\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left(\sum_{+,-}\sum_{s=\tau}^{\infty}2^{-\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)s}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}A_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{o}ffset}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

6.
Decomposition of the home form by CZ and
p
-energy coronas

In order to control the home form, we must pigeonhole the pairs of intervals

\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}
into a collection of
pairwise disjoint corona ‘boxes’ in which both
\sigma
-averages of
f
,
and Poisson-Energies of the measure pair
\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
,
are controlled. Then we split the home form into two forms according to this
decomposition, which we call the diagonal and far forms. But first we need
to construct the Calderón-Zygmund / Poisson-Energy corona decomposition.

Fix
\Gamma>1
and a large dyadic interval
T
. Define a sequence of
stopping times
\left\{\mathcal{F}_{n}\right\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
depending
on
T
,
\sigma
and
\omega
recursively as follows. Let
\mathcal{F}_{0}=\left\{T\right\}
. Given
\mathcal{F}_{n}
, define
\mathcal{F}_{n+1}

to consist of the
maximal

\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
intervals
I^{\prime}
for
which there is
I\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
with
I^{\prime}\subset I
and

(6.1)

\displaystyle\text{{either} }\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I^{\prime},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I^{\prime}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)}\right)^{p}\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(J,\omega\right)^{p}\frac{\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}}>\Gamma,

\displaystyle\text{{or} }\frac{1}{\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{I^{\prime}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[I\right]}^{\sigma}f\right|d\sigma>4\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{I}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[I\right]}^{\sigma}f\right|d\sigma,

where the
p
-energy
\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(J,\omega\right)
is defined by

(6.2)

\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(J,\omega\right)\equiv\left(\frac{1}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\int_{J}\left|x-\frac{1}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\int_{J}zd\omega\left(z\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\approx\left(\frac{1}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\int_{J}\left(\sum_{J^{\prime}\subset J}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J^{\prime}}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

where the equivalence follows from the square function Theorem
18
applied to
\mathbf{1}_{J}\left(Z-\frac{1}{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\int_{J}zd\omega\left(z\right)\right)
- the
p
-energy
\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(J,\omega\right)
is an
L^{p}
analogue of the energy
functional introduced in
[
LaSaUr2
]
. Also,
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[I\right]}^{\sigma}=\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}:\ K\subset I}\bigtriangleup_{K}^{\sigma}
, and
Z
denotes the identity function on
\mathbb{R}
,
Z\left(z\right)=z
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
, so that

\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\equiv\left\langle Z,h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right),\ \ \ \ \ \text{where }\left\langle Z,h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}=\int_{J}zd\omega\left(z\right)=\int_{J}\left(z-c\right)d\omega\left(z\right),\ \ \ c\in\mathbb{R}.

Set
\mathcal{F}\equiv\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}_{n}
, which we
refer to as the Calderón-Zygmund/Poisson-Energy stopping times for the
dyadic interval
T
, with measures
\sigma
and
\omega
. Note that
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
.

Notation 26
.

Denote the associated corona with top
F\in\mathcal{F}
by

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\equiv\left\{I\in\mathcal{D}:I\subset F\text{ and }I\not\subset F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}\text{ for any
}F^{\prime}\subsetneqq F\right\},

and the grandchildren at depth
m\in\mathbb{N}
of
F
in the tree
\mathcal{F}
by
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)
, with
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(1\right)}\left(F\right)
abbreviated to
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
. We
will also denote by
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F\right)\equiv\bigcup_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F\right)}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)
the union of
all
\mathcal{F}
-coronas at depth
k
below
F
, and we will denote by
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left[m\right]}\left(F\right)
(note the use
of square brackets in the exponent) the union of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(F\right)
for
0\leq k\leq m
. We will
consistantly use calligraphic font
\mathcal{C}
to denote coronas, and
fraktur font
\mathfrak{C}
to denote children, and will consistently use
superscripts
\left(m\right)
with parentheses to denote objects ‘
m

levels below’, and superscripts
\left[m\right]
with brackets to denote
objects ‘at most
m
levels below’.

Finally, we define

\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\equiv\sup_{G\in\mathcal{F}:\ G\supset F}E_{G}^{\sigma}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[\pi_{\mathcal{F}}G\right]}^{\sigma}f\right|,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }F\in\mathcal{F}.

The point of introducing the corona decomposition
\mathcal{D}\left[T\right]=\overset{\cdot}{\bigcup}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
is that, in each
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(F\right)\equiv\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
, we obtain control of both
the averages of projections of
f
,

(6.3)

E_{I}^{\sigma}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[F\right]}f\right|\equiv\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{I}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[F\right]}f\right|d\sigma\leq 4E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|,

by negating the second inequality in (
6.1
), as well as
control of the Stopping-Energy functional,

(6.4)

\mathfrak{X}_{F}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\equiv\sup_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus I}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{p}\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(I,\omega\right)^{p}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}},

by negating the first inequality in (
6.1
), i.e.

\frac{E_{I}^{\sigma}\left|\mathsf{P}_{F}f\right|}{E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|\mathsf{P}_{F}f\right|}\leq 4\text{ and }\mathfrak{X}_{F;p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\leq\Gamma,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all
}I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(F\right)\text{ and }F\in\mathcal{F}.

In particular, this inequality shows that the
Stopping-Energy
characteristic

\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F};p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\equiv\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathfrak{X}_{F;p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)

of
\sigma
and
\omega
with respect to the stopping times
\mathcal{F}
,
is dominated by the parameter
\Gamma
chosen in (
6.1
).

6.1.
Necessity of the
p
-energy condition

The proof of the stopping form bound will use the
L^{p}
-analogue of the
Poisson-energy characteristic introduced in
[
LaSaUr2
, (1.9) on page 3]
,

(6.5)

\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\equiv\sup_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\overset{\cdot}{\bigcup}_{r=1}^{\infty}I_{r}\subset I}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{p}\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(I_{r},\omega\right)^{p}\frac{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\text{ and its dual }\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega,\sigma\right),

where the supremum is taken over all pairwise disjoint subdecompositions of
an interval
I
into dyadic subintervals
I_{r}\in\mathcal{D}\left[I\right]
, and where the
p
-energy
\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(J,\omega\right)
is
defined in (
6.2
). Now we show the
p
-energy characteristic
\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
is controlled by the scalar
testing characteristic.

Lemma 27
.

For
1<p<\infty
we have

\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F},p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right).

Proof.

Let
\left\{I_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}
be a pairwise disjoint
decomposition of an interval
I
into subintervals
I_{r}
. We begin with
inequality (
3.5
) from Subsection
3.3
, namely
that for
x,y\in I_{r}\subset I
:

\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\left|x-y\right|\leq 2\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)-H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|.

Then taking the
p^{th}
power and integrating in
d\omega\left(x\right)

and
d\omega\left(y\right)
on both sides gives

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{p}\int_{I_{r}}\int_{I_{r}}\left|x-y\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)d\omega\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{p}\int_{I_{r}}\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)-H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)d\omega\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{I_{r}}\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)d\omega\left(y\right)+\int_{I_{r}}\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)d\omega\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right).

Using

\displaystyle\int_{I_{r}}\left|x-E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}Z\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{I_{r}}\left|\frac{1}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I_{r}}\left(x-y\right)d\omega\left(y\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I_{r}}\int_{I_{r}}\left|x-y\right|^{p}d\omega\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right),

we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{p}\int_{I_{r}}\left|x-E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}Z\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)+\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right).

Now summing in
r
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\left|I_{r}\right|}\right)^{p}\int_{I_{r}}\left|x-E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}Z\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{I}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)+\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\ .

The first inequality
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F},p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
in the
statement of the lemma follows directly from the
p
-energy stopping time
construction.

For
1<s<\infty
define

\displaystyle M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}h\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ x\in Q}\left(\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\omega}}\int_{Q}\left|h\right|^{s}d\omega\right)^{\frac{1}{s}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{p,s}\left(I,\omega\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(\int_{I}M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left[\left(Z-E_{Q}^{\omega}Z\right)\mathbf{1}_{I}\right]^{p}d\omega\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

and the
stronger

p
-energy constant,

\mathcal{E}_{p,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\equiv\sup_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\overset{\cdot}{\bigcup}_{r=1}^{\infty}I_{r}\subset I}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{p}\mathsf{E}_{p,s}\left(I_{r},\omega\right)^{p}\frac{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\text{ and its dual }\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime},s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right),

where the supremum is taken over all pairwise disjoint subdecompositions of
an interval
I
into dyadic subintervals
I_{r}\in\mathcal{D}\left[I\right]
.

Lemma 28
.

For
1<s<p<\infty
we have

\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F},p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\leq\mathcal{E}_{p,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right).

Proof.

Let
\left\{I_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}
be a pairwise disjoint
decomposition of an interval
I
into subintervals
I_{r}
. We begin with
inequality (
3.5
) from Subsection
3.3
, namely
that for
x,y\in I_{r}\subset I
:

\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\left|x-y\right|\leq 2\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)-H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|.

Then taking the average in
y
over
I_{r}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\left|x-E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}Z\right|=\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\left|\frac{1}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I_{r}}\left(x-y\right)d\omega\left(y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\frac{1}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I_{r}}\left|x-y\right|d\omega\left(y\right)\leq 2\frac{1}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)-H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|d\omega\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 2E_{I_{r}}^{\omega,y}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)-H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|,

where
E_{I_{r}}^{\omega,y}
denotes taking the
\omega
-average in
y

over the cube
I_{r}
. Then multiplying both sides by the indicator
\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}
and then applying the maximal operator
M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y},x}
in
x
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left[\left(Z-E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}Z\right)\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\right]\left(x\right)\leq 2M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y},x}E_{I_{r}}^{\omega,y}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\left(x\right)-H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\left(y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y},x}E_{I_{r}}^{\omega,y}\left|\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|+2M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y},x}E_{I_{r}}^{\omega,y}\left|\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)+2E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}\left|\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right|\leq 4M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right).

Now raise this to the power
p
and integrate with respect to
\omega
to
obtain,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{p}\int M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left[\left(Z-E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}Z\right)\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\right]\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4^{p}\int\left(M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\left(H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)\right]\left(x\right)\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\leq 4^{p}\int_{I_{r}}\left|\left(H\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)+\int_{I_{r}}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\sigma\left(y\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right).

Finally, summing in
r
produces,

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I\setminus I_{r}}\sigma\right)}{\left|I_{r}\right|}\right)^{p}\int M_{\omega,s}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left[\left(Z-E_{I_{r}}^{\omega}Z\right)\mathbf{1}_{I_{r}}\right]\left(x\right)^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{I}\left|H\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)+\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\ ,

which proves the inequality
\mathcal{E}_{p,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\mathcal{\ }
for
1<s<p<\infty
. The inequality
\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\leq\mathcal{E}_{p,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
is standard, and the
remaining inequalities in the statement of the lemma were proved in the
earlier lemma.

6.2.
Consequences of the CZ and
p
-energy corona decomposition

If we
assume
the finiteness of the energy characteristic
\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
in (
6.5
) (which is often
referred to as the
energy condition
), and if we take
\Gamma>\max\left\{8\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right),8\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right\}
in (
6.1
),
we obtain a
\sigma
-Carleson
9
9

9

Such conditions are more commonly referred to as
\sigma
-sparse conditions
nowadays.
condition for the Calderón-Zygmund/Poisson-Energy stopping
times
\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Gamma}\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\min\left\{\frac{\int_{F^{\prime}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{F}f\right|d\sigma}{E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|\mathsf{P}_{F}f\right|},\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(F^{\prime},\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus F^{\prime}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)}\right)^{p}\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(F^{\prime},\omega\right)^{p}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\omega},\frac{1}{\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{F^{\prime}}\left|M_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\right|^{p}d\omega\right\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq\left(\frac{1}{4}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}}{\Gamma}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}+\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}}{\Gamma}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\right)<\frac{1}{2}\left|F\right|_{\sigma},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }F\in\mathcal{F}\ ,

since

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|M_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\right|^{p}d\omega\leq C_{p}\int_{F}\left|\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\right|^{p}d\omega\leq C_{p}\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\left|F\right|_{\sigma},

which can then be iterated to obtain
geometric decay in generations
,

(6.6)

\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)}\left|G\right|_{\sigma}\leq C_{\delta}2^{-\delta m}\left|F\right|_{\sigma},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }m\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }F\in\mathcal{F}\ .

In addition we obtain the quasiorthogonality inequality, given as (3) in the
definition of stopping data above,

(6.7)

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{p}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|f\right|^{p}d\sigma,\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<\infty,

which follows easily from that in
[
LaSaShUr3
]
,
[
SaShUr7
]
or
[
LaWi
]
, or equivalently from the Carleson embedding theorem, upon noting
that
E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|\mathsf{P}_{F}f\right|=E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f-E_{F}^{\sigma}f\right|\leq 2E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|
. In fact the reader can easily verify that the triple
\left(C_{0},\mathcal{F},\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\right)
constitutes
stopping
data
for the function
f\in L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)
for some constant
C_{0}
depending on
\Gamma
, and hence satisfies the stronger
quasiorthogonality property (
3.7
) as well.

The finiteness of the energy characteristic
\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
will be needed both to control the Stopping-Energy
characteristic
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
, which is needed to
control the stopping form, and to enforce (
6.6
), that is in turn
needed to control the far, paraproduct and stopping forms. Finally, we can
appeal to Lemma
27
for

(6.8)

\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right),\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<\infty,

that controls
\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
by the testing
characteristic for the
Hilbert transform
. Unfortunately this simple
inequality fails, even with a Muckenhoupt characteristic added to the right
hand side, for most other Calderón-Zygmund operators in place of the
Hilbert transform, including Riesz transforms in higher dimensions, see
[
SaShUr11
]
and
[
Saw5
]
, and this failure limits the current proof to
essentially just the Hilbert transform and similar operators on the real
line as in
[
SaShUr11
]
.

6.2.1.
Pigeonholing in corona boxes

Now we can pigeonhole the pairs of intervals arising in the sum defining the
below form. Given the corona decomposition of
\mathcal{D}
according to the
Calderón-Zygmund stopping times
\mathcal{F}
constructed above, we
define the analogous decomposition of,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\equiv\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}:J\subset_{\tau}I\right\}=\bigcup_{F,G\in\mathcal{F}:\ G\subset F}\left[\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)\right]\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\bigcup_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left[\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\right]\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\right\}\bigcup\left\{\bigcup_{F,G\in\mathcal{F}:\ G\subsetneqq F}\left[\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)\right]\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\ .

Then we consider the corresponding decomposition of the home form into
diagonal and far forms,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{h}ome}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}+\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right).

We next decompose the
\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}
form into corona pieces using
\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}\equiv\left[\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)\right]\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F,G\in\mathcal{F}:\ G\subsetneqq F}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right):\ J\subset_{\tau}I}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F,G\in\mathcal{F}:\ G\subsetneqq F}\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{F,G\in\mathcal{F}:\ G\subsetneqq F}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\text{where }\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}.

Now for
m>\tau
and
F\in\mathcal{F}
we define

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,m}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)}\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(F^{\prime}\right)}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right):\ J\subset_{\tau}I}\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}.

We will now control the
\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}
form for
1<p<\infty
in the remainder
of this section, and finally control the
diagonal
form in the last
two sections.

6.3.
Far form

Here we will control the
\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}
form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)
by quadratic local testing and the extended energy
characteristic
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
. Recall that the
\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}
form is defined by

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,m}\left(f,g\right),

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,m}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)}\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}^{F,G}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right):\ J\subset_{\tau}I}\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}.

Thus we can write

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(F\right)}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right):\ J\subset_{\tau}I}\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{G\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ G\subsetneqq I\text{ and }J\subset_{\tau}I}\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega},

which we will usually consider with the dummy variable
G
replaced by
F
,

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I\in\left(F,T\right]\text{ and }J\subset_{\tau}I}\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}.

Given any collection
\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{D}
of intervals, and a
dyadic interval
J
, we define the corresponding Haar projection
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\omega}
and its localization
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{H};J}^{\omega}
to
J
by

(6.9)

\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\omega}=\sum_{H\in\mathcal{H}}\bigtriangleup_{H}^{\omega}\text{ and }\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{H};J}^{\omega}=\sum_{H\in\mathcal{H}:\ H\subset J}\bigtriangleup_{H}^{\omega}\ .

Recall that given any interval
F\in\mathcal{D}
, we defined the
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)
-Whitney
collection
\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)
of
F
to be the set
of dyadic subintervals
W\subset F
that are maximal with respect to the
property that
W\subset_{r,\varepsilon}F
, and hence form a pairwise
disjoint decomposition of
F
. Recall also that
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
denotes the smallest constant
in the ‘extended energy’ inequality (
1.7
), i.e.

\int\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(W,\Phi_{F}f\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(W\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|^{2}Z\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }f\geq 0,

where
\mathcal{F}
is the collection of stopping times associated with
f
,
and

\Phi_{F}f\equiv\mathbb{E}_{F}^{\sigma}f+f_{F}=\mathbb{E}_{F}^{\sigma}f+\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f.

There is a similar definition of the dual characteristic
\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)
. The
Intertwining Proposition will control the following Intertwining form,

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{I}nter}}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\ ,

whose difference from
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)
is

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)-\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{I}nter}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{I\in\left(F,T\right]}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\text{ and }J\subset_{\tau}I}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle-\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{I\in\left(F,T\right]}\ \sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{I\in\left(F,T\right]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\\
\ell\left(J\right)\geq\ell\left(F\right)-\tau\text{ and }J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}.

Just as for the comparable form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}omp}}\left(f,g\right)
, this difference form is controlled by

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)-\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{I}nter}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{I\in\left(F,T\right]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\\
\ell\left(J\right)\geq\ell\left(F\right)-\tau\text{ and
}J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{A}_{p}^{\ell^{2}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{WBP}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

which is also bounded by
\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}
.

Definition 29
.

A collection
\mathcal{F}
of dyadic intervals is
\sigma
-Carleson
if

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset S}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\leq C_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\sigma\right)\left|S\right|_{\sigma},\ \ \ \ \ S\in\mathcal{F}.

The constant
C_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\sigma\right)
is referred to as the
Carleson norm of
\mathcal{F}
.

We now show that the extended energy inequality (
1.7
), together
with quadratic interval testing, suffices to prove the Intertwining
Proposition.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be any subset of
\mathcal{D}
. For any
J\in\mathcal{D}
, we define
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{0}J
to be the smallest
F\in\mathcal{F}

that contains
J
. Then for
s\geq 1
, we recursively define
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{s}J
to be the smallest
F\in\mathcal{F}
that
strictly

contains
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{s-1}J
. This definition satisfies
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{s+t}J=\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{s}\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{t}J
for all
s,t\geq 0
and
J\in\mathcal{D}
. In particular
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{s}J=\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{s}F
where
F=\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{0}J
. In the
special case
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{D}
we often suppress the subscript
\mathcal{F}
and simply write
\pi^{s}
for
\pi_{\mathcal{D}}^{s}
.
Finally, for
F\in\mathcal{F}
, we write
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\equiv\left\{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{1}F^{\prime}=F\right\}
for the collection of
\mathcal{F}
-children of

F
.

Proposition 30

(The Intertwining Proposition)
.

Suppose
1<p<\infty
and
\sigma,\omega
are
locally finite positive Borel measures on
\mathbb{R}
. Then if
\mathcal{F}

is the collection of stopping times for
f\in L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)

as above, we have

\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\left(\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

Proof.

We write the left hand side of the display above as

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}\equiv\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}f_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}\ ,

where

g_{F}=\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\text{ and }f_{F}\equiv\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\ .

Note that
g_{F}
is supported in
F
, and that
f_{F}
is constant on
F
.
We note that the intervals
I
occurring in this sum are linearly and
consecutively ordered by inclusion, along with the intervals
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
that contain
F
. More precisely, we can write

F\equiv F_{0}\subsetneqq F_{1}\subsetneqq F_{2}\subsetneqq...\subsetneqq F_{n}\subsetneqq F_{n+1}\subsetneqq...F_{N}

where
F_{m}=\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F
for all
m\geq 1
. We can also write

F=F_{0}\subsetneqq I_{1}\subsetneqq I_{2}\subsetneqq...\subsetneqq I_{k}\subsetneqq I_{k+1}\subsetneqq...\subsetneqq I_{K}=F_{N}

where
I_{k}=\pi_{\mathcal{D}}^{k}F
for all
k\geq 1
, and by convention
we set
I_{0}=F
. There is a (unique) subsequence
\left\{k_{m}\right\}_{m=1}^{N}
such that

F_{m}=I_{k_{m}},\ \ \ \ \ 1\leq m\leq N.

Recall that

f_{F}\left(x\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\left(I_{k}\right)_{F}}\left(x\right)\bigtriangleup_{I_{k}}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{I_{k}\setminus I_{k-1}}\left(x\right)\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{\infty}\bigtriangleup_{I_{\ell}}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right).

Assume now that
k_{m}\leq k<k_{m+1}
. Using a telescoping sum, we compute
that for

x\in I_{k+1}\setminus I_{k}\subset F_{m+1}\setminus F_{m},

we have

\left|\sum_{\ell=k+2}^{\infty}\bigtriangleup_{I_{\ell}}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\theta I_{k+2}}^{\sigma}f-\mathbb{E}_{I_{K}}^{\sigma}f\right|\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{F_{m+1}}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\ .

Note that
f_{F}
is constant on
F
and that

\displaystyle\left|f_{F}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}_{F_{m+1}}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F_{m+1}\setminus F_{m}}=\left(\mathbb{E}_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F}+\sum_{m=0}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F}+\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset F^{\prime}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\setminus F^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F}+\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset F^{\prime}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\setminus F^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F}+\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset F^{\prime}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}}\ \mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F}+\Phi\ \mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \text{\ for all }F\in\mathcal{F},

where

\Phi\equiv\sum_{F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\ \alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F^{\prime\prime}\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F^{\prime\prime}}\ .

Now we write

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}f_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}f_{F}\right),g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}+\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right),g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}\equiv I+II.

Then quadratic interval testing, the square function inequalities in Theorem
18
, and quasi-orthogonality together with the fact that
f_{F}

is a constant on
F
bounded by
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
, give

(6.10)

\displaystyle\left|I\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}f_{F}\right)\left(x\right)\ g_{F}\left(x\right)\ d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\left|\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}\right)\left(x\right)\ g_{F}\left(x\right)\right|\ d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}\right)\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}\ \sqrt{\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|g_{F}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}\ d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \left\|\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\ \left\|\left|\left\{g_{F}\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim C_{\mathcal{F}}\left(\sigma\right)\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

Now
\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}
is supported outside
F
, and each
J
in the
Haar support of
g_{F}=\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g
is either in
\mathcal{N}_{r}\left(F\right)=\left\{J\in\mathcal{D}\left[F\right]:\ell\left(J\right)\geq 2^{-r}\ell\left(F\right)\right\}
, in which case the desired bound for term
I
is
straightforward, or
J
is
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)
-deeply embedded
in
F
, i.e.
J\subset_{r,\varepsilon}F
, and so
J\subset_{r,\varepsilon}W
for some
W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)
. Since the corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
is connected, it follows that
W\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
if
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}g
is nonvanishing. Thus with
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\equiv\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\setminus\mathcal{N}_{r}\left(F\right)
we
can apply
15
to obtain

\displaystyle\left|II\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right)\left(x\right)\ \mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\ d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\ \mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right)\left(x\right)\ \mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\ d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\ \left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right)\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}\

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\sqrt{\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\ \left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}}\ d\omega\left(x\right),

which is at most

\displaystyle\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\ \left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right)\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right\}^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\ \left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right\}^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}.

The second factor is at most
C_{p}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}
by Theorem
18
.

Then we use the Energy Lemma on the first factor to obtain that its
p^{th}

power is at most,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\ \left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(W,\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(W\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|Z\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\ \left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(W,\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}\Phi_{F}f\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(W\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|Z\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},

where the last line follows from the definition of the extended energy
characteristic.

This completes the proof of the Intertwining Proposition
30
.

Thus we have the following controls of the
\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}
form,

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{A}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathcal{WBP}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}}\left(f,g\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{g}lob}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

In Section
9
below, we will show that the extended energy
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)

characteristic is controlled by the norm characteristic
\mathfrak{N}_{H,p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
, and hence is a necessary condition.

7.
Reduction of the diagonal form by the NTV reach

We first apply the clever ‘NTV reach’ of
[
NTV4
]
, which splits the
diagonal form

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{J}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{J}},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega},

into a paraproduct and stopping form,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{J}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle+\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{J}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus I_{J}},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right).

7.1.
Paraproduct form

Here we bound the paraproduct form,

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left\langle\left(E_{J^{\ast}}^{\sigma}f\right)H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega},

for
1<p<\infty
, where
J^{\ast}=I_{J}
where
I
is the smallest
interval in the Haar support of
f
for which
J
is
\tau
-deeply embedded
in
I
. Define
\widetilde{g}=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\frac{E_{J^{\ast}}^{\sigma}f}{E_{F}^{\sigma}f}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g
and note
that
\left|E_{J^{\ast}}^{\sigma}f\right|\lesssim\left|E_{F}^{\sigma}f\right|
since
J^{\ast}=I_{J}
is
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}

because
I
is in the Haar support of
f
. Then we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}\left(f,g\right)\right|=\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)\right|=\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left\langle\left(E_{J^{\ast}}^{\sigma}f\right)H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F},\frac{E_{J^{\ast}}^{\sigma}f}{E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|=\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F},\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}\widetilde{g}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\left\langle\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F},\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}\widetilde{g}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\left(x\right)\ \mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}\widetilde{g}\left(x\right)\ d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}\widetilde{g}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ d\omega\left(x\right),

and we can write

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}\widetilde{g}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}.

We claim the following inequalities for all
1<p<\infty
,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\sigma\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\sigma\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\sigma\left(x\right)\lesssim\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{p}\left|F\right|_{\sigma},

\displaystyle\text{and in particular }\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{p}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}.

Since by quasiorthogonality we have
\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)^{p}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|f\right|^{p}d\sigma
, the inequality (
7.1
) will then yield

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|f\right|^{p}d\sigma.

If we now combine this inequality with the standard square function estimate,

(7.2)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}\widetilde{g}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\widetilde{g}\right|^{p^{\prime}}d\omega,

and the inequality
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\widetilde{g}\right|^{p^{\prime}}d\omega\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|g\right|^{p^{\prime}}d\omega
, which follows from
\left|E_{J^{\ast}}^{\sigma}f\right|\lesssim\left|E_{F}^{\sigma}f\right|
,
we obtain

\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}ara}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},

which is the desired estimate for the paraproduct form.

Now we turn to proving (
7.1
). The local quadratic testing condition
gives the first inequality in (
7.1
). Indeed, with
\mathcal{F}=\left\{I_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
and
a_{i}=\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(I_{i}\right)
, we have using that the projection
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}
is supported in
F
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\left\{\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left(a_{i}\mathbf{1}_{I_{i}}H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{i}}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}^{p}\leq\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\left\|\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left(a_{i}\mathbf{1}_{I_{i}}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}^{p}d\sigma\left(x\right).

The second inequality in (
7.1
) is (
3.9
) in Theorem
17
with
\kappa=0
, and this completes the proof of (
7.1
) and hence the control of the paraproduct form for
1<p<\infty
.

8.
Stopping form

To control the stopping form

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{J}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left\langle H_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus I_{J}},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

we assume that the Haar supports of
f
and
g
are contained in
\digamma\cap\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}}
for some large but finite
and connected subset
\digamma
of the grid
\mathcal{D}
, and let
\mathcal{F}
denote the Calderón-Zygmund stopping times for
f
with top

T
.

Remark 31
