.

Note that for each
F\in\mathcal{F}
, the sums over
I
and
J
above
depend on
F
through the corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
, and the argument
\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus I_{J}}
of the Hilbert transform
also depends on
F
.

The estimate we prove here is

(8.1)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<4,

for
f\in L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)
and
g\in L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\omega\right)

with Haar supports in
\digamma
, and where the stopping times
\mathcal{F}

that arise in the definition of
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)
are the Calderon-Zygmund stopping times for
f
. The
restriction
\frac{4}{3}<p
arises from treating the dual stopping form.
Control of the stopping form will require the most formidable estimates yet,
despite that only the simplest of characteristics is used, namely the scalar
testing characteristic
\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
. Recall that the inequality
\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\leq\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
is trivial.

The key technical estimate needed for (
8.1
) is the Quadratic
L^{p}
-Stopping Child Lemma below which controls off-diagonal terms, and
which has its roots in the ‘straddling’ lemmas of M. Lacey in
[
Lac
, Lemmas
3.19 and 3.16]
. To prove this lemma, we will need a Corona Martingale
Comparison Principle that replaces a martingale difference sequence with
another having differences comparable in norm. Then we use a square function
generalization of Lacey’s upside down corona construction in the dual tree
decomposition to finish the proof with a somewhat lengthy argument, in which
the restriction
\frac{4}{3}<p<4
arises.

8.1.
Heuristics

In order to explain the origin of the Quadratic
L^{p}
-Stopping Child Lemma
and Lemma
38
at the end of the paper, we point out a
significant obstacle arising from the lack of orthogonality when
p\neq 2
,
and which leads to the restriction
\frac{4}{3}<p<4
. The following short
discussion is intended to be heuristic and without precise notation and
definitions. The reader can safely skip this subsubsection and proceed
directly to the next subsubsection on the dual tree decomposition.

We now
fix
functions
f\in L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)
and
g\in L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\omega\right)
, along with the Calderón-Zygmund
stopping times
\mathcal{F}
for
f
, and of course the
p
-energy stopping
times for the measure pair
\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
. The stopping
form

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(f,g\right)

is already a ‘quadratic’ form in the sense that it is a one parameter sum
over
F
, rather than the two parameter sum over
F
and
G
that appears
for example in the below form,

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)=\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\sigma}f,\sum_{G\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)}^{\omega}g\right)=\sum_{F,G\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(G\right)}^{\omega}g\right).

The basic idea for controlling the stopping form in
[
Lac
, Lemmas 3.19 and
3.16]
when
p=2
, is to construct additional bottom-up stopping times
\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]
within each corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
that control energy associated with the Haar support of
g
, and then using certain ‘straddling’ lemmas, to reduce control of the
resulting bilinear forms

\sum_{A,B\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(B\right)}^{\omega}g\right)

within each corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
, to their
‘quadratic’ counterparts

\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}g\right)=\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A}\left(f,g\right),

where the norm of the forms
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A}
are small compared to the norm of
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}
. At this point one uses the Quasi-Orthogonality Argument
in
[
Lac
, page 6]
to control the entire sum of the iterated ‘quadratic’
forms, namely

(8.2)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A}\right\|\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\omega}g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sup_{\left(F,A\right)\in\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A}\right\|\right)\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\omega}g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sup_{\left(F,A\right)\in\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A}\right\|\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\leq\varepsilon\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\right\|\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}.

Then one can finish by recursion as in
[
Lac
]
, or by absorption as in

[
Saw7
]
, which formally (ignoring the nature of the smallness factor and
just inserting a small
\varepsilon>0
) becomes

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\right\|

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)\right|}{\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}+\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A}\left(f,g\right)\right|}{\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}+\varepsilon\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\right\|\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}}{\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}}=C+\varepsilon\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\right\|,

which yields
\left\|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\right\|\leq\frac{C}{1-\varepsilon}
.

Unfortunately, the inequality (
8.2
) fails to generalize for all
1<p<\infty
, and we must arrange matters so as to avoid its use when
p\neq 2
. This will be accomplished by applying the above heuristic to more
general forms

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{Q}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\left(Q\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\left(Q\right)}^{\omega}g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right],Q}\left(f,g\right),

in which a larger collection of stopping times
\mathcal{Q\supset F}
is
used in order to introduce an additional layer of stopping times
\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]=\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)

within each corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
. Then we can
interpret the iterated quadratic form

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],A}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{d}iag}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\omega}g\right)

as simply the form

(8.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q^{\prime}\in\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)\left[F\right],Q^{\prime}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q^{\prime}\in\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)\left[F\right],Q^{\prime}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)\left[F\right]}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)\left[F\right]}\left(Q^{\prime}\right)}^{\omega}g\right),

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{where }\left(\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)\left[F\right]=\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right].

This will be shown below to circumvent use of the Quasi-Orthogonality
Argument in the restricted range
1<p<4
(which is then further restricted
to
\frac{4}{3}<p
when treating the dual stopping form). See also the
section on Concluding Remarks at the end of the paper for more discussion on
this point. We will be using special stopping collections
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{A}
constructed using the upside down corona of Lacey
[
Lac
]
,
but adapted to
p\neq 2
, and we now turn to this construction.

8.2.
Dual tree decomposition

To control the stopping form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)
, we need to introduce further corona decompositions within each corona
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
to which we can apply the
L^{p}
-Stopping Child Lemma. These coronas will be associated to stopping
intervals
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
, whose construction, following
[
Saw7
]
,
uses a dual tree decomposition originating with M. Lacey in
[
Lac
]
.
However, our stopping criteria will be different when
p\neq 2
, and the
arguments more involved.

Definition 32
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a tree with root
o
.

(1)

Let
P\left(\alpha\right)\equiv\left\{\beta\in\mathcal{T}:\beta\succeq\alpha\right\}
and
S\left(\alpha\right)\equiv\left\{\beta\in\mathcal{T}:\beta\preceq\alpha\right\}
denote the predessor and
successor sets of
\alpha\in\mathcal{T}
.

(2)

A
geodesic

\mathfrak{g}
is a maximal linearly ordered subset
of
\mathcal{T}
. A finite geodesic
\mathfrak{g}
is an interval
\mathfrak{g}=\left[\alpha,\beta\right]=P\left(\beta\right)\setminus S\left(\alpha\right)
, and an infinite geodesic is an interval
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}\setminus P\left(\alpha\right)
for some
\alpha\in\mathfrak{g}
. Intervals
\left(\alpha,\beta\right)
,
\left(\alpha,\beta\right]
and
\left[\alpha,\beta\right]
are defined similarly.

(3)

A
stopping time
10
10

10

This different definition of stopping time used here, is that used in the
theory of trees, but should cause no confusion with the other definition we
use elsewhere, that a stopping time is any subset of
\mathcal{T}
.

T
for
a tree
\mathcal{T}
is a subset
T\subset\mathcal{T}
such that

S\left(\beta\right)\cap S\left(\beta^{\prime}\right)=\emptyset\text{
for all }\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in T\text{ with }\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}.

(4)

A sequence
\left\{T_{n}\right\}_{n=0}^{N}
of stopping times
T_{n}

is
decreasing
if, for every
\beta\in T_{n+1}
with
0\leq n<N
,
there is
\beta^{\prime}\in T_{n}
such that
S\left(\beta\right)\subset S\left(\beta^{\prime}\right)
. We think of such a sequence as
getting further from the root as
n
increases.

(5)

For
T
a stopping time in
\mathcal{T}
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{T}
,
we define

\left[T,\alpha\right)\equiv\bigcup_{\beta\in T}\left[\beta,\alpha\right),

where the interval
\left[\beta,\alpha\right)=\emptyset
unless
\beta\prec\alpha
. In the case
\left[T,\alpha\right)=\emptyset
, we write
\alpha\preceq T
, and in the case
\left[T,\alpha\right)\not=\emptyset
, we write
\alpha\succ T
. The set
\left[T,\alpha\right)
can be
thought of as the set of points in the tree
\mathcal{T}
that ‘lie between’

T
and
\alpha
but are strictly less than
\alpha
. We also define
\left(T,\alpha\right)
,
\left(T,\alpha\right]
and
\left[T,\alpha\right]
in similar fashion.

(6)

For any
\alpha\in\mathcal{T}
, we define the set of its
children

\mathfrak{C}_{T}\left(\alpha\right)
to consist of the
maximal
elements
\beta\in\mathcal{T}
such that
\beta\prec\alpha
.

In the finite tree pictured below, downward arrows point to small tree
elements, and we have for example,

\displaystyle\gamma\prec\beta\prec\alpha,\ \ \ P\left(\gamma\right)=\left\{\gamma,\beta,\alpha,o\right\}=\left[\gamma,o\right],\ \ \ \left(\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},o\right]=\left\{\beta^{\prime\prime},\alpha,o\right\},

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and }S\left(\alpha\right)=\left\{\alpha,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime\prime},\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime\prime},\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\}.

\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}&&&&o&&&&\\
&&&\swarrow&\downarrow&\searrow&&&\\
&&\alpha^{\prime}&&\alpha&&\alpha^{\prime\prime}&&\\
&&&\swarrow&\downarrow&\searrow&&&\\
&&\beta&&\beta^{\prime}&&\beta^{\prime\prime}&&\\
&\swarrow&\downarrow&&&&\downarrow&\searrow&\\
\gamma&&\gamma^{\prime}&&&&\gamma^{\prime\prime}&&\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\end{array}\right]

Lemma
33
below will create a set stopping times for any function
\nu:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow L^{p}\left(\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{T}\right);\omega\right)
with finite support. It might be useful to point out the
application we have in mind. Namely, we will take
\mathcal{T}
to be a
connected subset of the grid
\mathcal{D}
, where the root
o
is a dyadic
interval
T\in\mathcal{T}
, and where
J\preccurlyeq I
is defined to hold
if and only if
J\subset I
(i.e. the symbols
\preccurlyeq
and
\subset

are consistent). For any subset
\Lambda\subset\mathcal{T}
, we will
consider the sequence-valued function
\nu_{\Lambda}:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow L^{p}\left(\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{T}\right);\omega\right)

defined by

(8.4)

\nu_{\Lambda}\left(I\right)\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\left\{\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{I\right\}}\left(J\right)\right\}_{J\in\mathcal{T}}&\text{ if }&I\in\Lambda\\
0&\text{ if }&I\notin\Lambda\end{array}\right.\ ,

i.e.
\nu_{\Lambda}\left(I\right)
is the sequence
\left\{f_{J}\left(x\right)\right\}_{J\in\mathcal{T}}
where
f_{J}\left(x\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)&\text{ if }&J=I\in\Lambda\\
0&\text{ if }&J\neq I\end{array}\right.
and is the zero sequence otherwise. We define the dual integration
operator
I^{\ast}
on
\nu
by
I^{\ast}\nu\left(\alpha\right)\equiv\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{T}:\ \beta\prec\alpha}\nu\left(\beta\right)
. More generally, for any subset
\Omega
of the tree
\mathcal{T}
, we
define

I^{\Omega}\nu\equiv\sum_{\beta\in\Omega}\nu\left(\beta\right),

in which case
I^{\ast}\nu\left(\alpha\right)=I^{S\left(\alpha\right)}\nu
. Note that in the application setting discussed above, we have

\left\|I^{\ast}\nu_{\Lambda}\left(I\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}=\left\|\left(\sum_{J\in\Lambda:\ J\subset I}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}^{p}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{J\in\Lambda:\ J\subset I}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right).

Here is the dual stopping time lemma that abstracts and extends that of M.
Lacey in
[
Lac
]
to
p\neq 2
. We state this lemma for a tree with
bounded numbers of children, but we will only use the case of a dyadic tree,
which has at most two children.

Lemma 33
.

Let
\left(\mathcal{T},\preccurlyeq\right)
be a tree with
root
o
and
M\equiv\sup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{T}}\#\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)<\infty
, and suppose
\nu:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow L^{p}\left(\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{T}\right);\omega\right)
is
nontrivial with finite support, and that
T_{0}
is the stopping time
consisting of the minimal tree elements in the support of
\nu
. Fix
\Gamma>1
11
11

11

This is not necessarily the same
\Gamma
as used in the Calderón-Zygmund stopping time construction (
6.1
).
. If there is
no element
\alpha\in\mathcal{T}
with

\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\alpha\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}>\Gamma^{p}\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{T}:\ \beta\prec\alpha}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p},

we say the tree is
\Gamma
-irreducible. Otherwise, there is a unique
increasing sequence
\left\{T_{n}\right\}_{n=0}^{N+1}
, with
T_{N+1}=\left\{o\right\}
, of stopping times
T_{n}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
with
n\leq N
,

(8.5)

\displaystyle\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\alpha\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\Gamma^{p}\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\alpha}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }\alpha\in T_{n}\ ;

\displaystyle\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\gamma\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma^{p}\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p},\text{ \ \ \ \ for all }\gamma\in\left[T_{n-1},\alpha\right)\text{ with }\alpha\in T_{n}\ ;

\displaystyle\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(o\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma^{p}\sum_{\beta\in T_{N}:\ \beta\prec\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}\ .

Moreover, this unique sequence
\left\{T_{n}\right\}_{n=0}^{N+1}

satisfies the following inequalities,

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|I^{\left(T_{n-1},\alpha\right)}\nu\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\alpha}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\left(\Gamma^{p}-1\right)^{\natural},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }\alpha\in T_{n}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ 1\leq n\leq N+1,

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|I^{\left(T_{n-1},\gamma\right)}\nu\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\left(\Gamma^{p}-1\right)^{\natural},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }\gamma\in\left(S\left(\alpha\right)\setminus\left\{\alpha\right\}\right)\setminus\bigcup_{\beta\in T_{n-1}}S\left(\beta\right),

where
\Theta^{\natural}\equiv\max\left\{\Theta^{\frac{p}{2}},\Theta\right\}
for any
\Theta>0
. When
p\geq 2
, we may drop the constant
C_{p}
and the
\natural
in the exponent.

Proof.

If
T_{n}
is already defined, let
T_{n+1}
consist of all minimal points
\alpha\in\mathcal{T}
satisfying

(8.7)

\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\alpha\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}>\Gamma^{p}\sum_{\beta\in T_{n}:\ \beta\succ\alpha}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}\ ,

provided at least one such point
\alpha
exists. If not then set
N=n
and
define
T_{N+1}\equiv\left\{o\right\}
. It is easy to see that the
sequence
\left\{T_{n}\right\}_{n=0}^{N+1}
so constructed is an
increasing sequence of stopping times that satisfies (
8.5
), and
is unique with these properties.

Note that for
q\geq 1
, we have

(8.8)

\displaystyle\left(a+b\right)^{q}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle a^{q}+b^{q}\text{ for }a,b>0\text{ and }q\geq 1,

\displaystyle\text{i.e. }b^{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(a+b\right)^{q}-a^{q}\text{ for }a,b>0\text{
and }q\geq 1.

Thus for
p\geq 2
, we have
q=\frac{p}{2}\geq 1
and the first line in (
) holds since using Lemma
23
,

\displaystyle\left\|I^{\left(T_{n-1},\alpha\right)}\nu\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left\|I^{\left(T_{n-1},\gamma\right)}\nu\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\gamma\right)-\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\gamma\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}-\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left\|\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{by (\ref{q1})},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\Gamma^{p}\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}-\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\Gamma^{p}-1\right)\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}=\left(\Gamma^{p}-1\right)\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\alpha}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}.

In the case
1<p<2
, we must work harder since (
8.8
) fails when
q<1
.
In fact we now use Lemma
25
from the section on preliminaries to show
that the first line in (
) holds. Indeed, from Lemmas
23
and
25
, and using that the maximal elements in
\left(T_{n-1},\alpha\right)
are the children
\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|I^{\left(T_{n-1},\alpha\right)}\nu\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\left\|\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\alpha}I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}\leq\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\frac{\left\|I^{\left(T_{n-1},\gamma\right)}\nu\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\left\|\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\frac{\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\gamma\right)-\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\left\|\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\frac{\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\gamma\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\left\|\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}-1\right)^{\natural},

by (
3.24
), which equals, again using Lemma
23
,

C_{p}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\frac{\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\gamma\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}{\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\preccurlyeq\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}}-1\right)^{\natural}\leq MC_{p}\left(\Gamma^{p}-1\right)^{\natural}=C_{p,M}\left(\Gamma^{p}-1\right)^{\natural}.

The same arguments prove the second line in (
), since
\gamma\in\left(S\left(\alpha\right)\setminus\left\{\alpha\right\}\right)\setminus\bigcup_{\beta\in T_{n-1}}S\left(\beta\right)
was

not
chosen by the stopping criterion in the first line of (
8.5
), and hence

\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\gamma\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}\leq\Gamma^{p}\sum_{\beta\in T_{n-1}:\ \beta\prec\gamma}\left\|I^{\ast}\nu\left(\beta\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\ell^{2};\omega\right)}^{p}.

We can now proceed as above, and this completes the proof of Lemma
33
.

8.3.
Corona Martingale Comparison Principle

Suppose
\mu
is a locally finite positive Borel measure on the real line
\mathbb{R}
, that
\mathcal{L}\subset\mathcal{D}
with top interval
T
,
and that
\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}
is the associated collection of coronas. For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
define

\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k}^{\mu}\equiv\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}\text{ where }\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(F\right)}^{\mu}\equiv\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(F\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}.

Then as shown in
[
SaWi
, see the section on square functions and vector-valued
inequalities]
, the sequence
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k}^{\mu}g\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a martingale difference sequence of an
L^{p}
bounded martingale for any
g\in L^{p}\left(\mu\right)
. We will
refer to such a martingale on the real line as an
L^{p}
-
\mathcal{L}
martingale
. We define
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(F\right)
to be the set of
\ell
-grandchildren of
F
in
the tree
\mathcal{L}
, and

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(F\right)\equiv\bigcup_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(F\right)}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(F^{\prime}\right)\text{
and }\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[m\right]}\left(F\right)\equiv\bigcup_{\ell=0}^{m}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(F\right).

The comparison principle for corona martingales is a transplantation theorem
relying on the structure of corona martingales for its success. Variants of
this type of comparison principle for martingale differences arose in work
of J. Zinn almost four decades ago
[
Zin
]
. See also Burkholder
[
Bur
]

for related inequalities.

Proposition 34

(Corona Martingale Comparison Principle)
.

Let
1<p<\infty
, and
m,N\in\mathbb{N}
with
m>N
. Let
\mu
be a locally finite positive Borel measure on
\mathbb{R}
, let
\mathcal{L}\subset\mathcal{D}
with top interval
T
, and suppose that
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k}g\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k}b\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are martingale difference
sequences of
L^{p}
-
\mathcal{L}
martingales with
\int_{T}gd\mu=\int_{T}bd\mu=0
. Suppose moreover, that
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}^{\mu}g=0
for
0\leq k\leq m-1
. Then we have

(8.9)

\left\|\left|\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k}^{\mu}g\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}\lesssim mNM_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)\ \max_{0\leq s\leq N}\left\|\left|\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k+s}^{\mu}b\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)},

where

(8.10)

M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)\equiv\sup_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\frac{\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\bigcup_{K\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\mathcal{D}\left(K\right)}^{\mu}g\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}}{\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}}.

Remark 35
.

A crucial feature of the proof, peculiar to corona martingales, is that
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b
is constant on the support of
\mathsf{P}_{\bigcup_{K\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\mathcal{D}\left(K\right)}^{\mu}g
when
m>N
.

We will frequently use the square function in Theorem
18
in
the proof of Proposition
34
, which in particular implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}f\right\}_{L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}=\left\|\left(\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}f\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}f\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}=\left\|\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}f\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)},

for any
f\in L^{p}\left(\mu\right)
and
L\in\mathcal{L}
. Note that
whenever the supports of scalar functions
f_{n}\left(x\right)
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
are pairwise disjoint for
x\in\mathbb{R}
, then at most one
f_{n}\left(x\right)\neq 0
for any fixed
x
, and so we have

\left|\left\{f_{n}\left(x\right)\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}=\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|f_{n}\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|f_{n}\left(x\right)\right|=\left|\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f_{n}\left(x\right)\right|,\text{\ \ \ \ \ for
all }x\in\mathbb{R}.

In particular this applies to the sequence
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}f\right\}_{L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}
for each fixed
m
.

Proof.

We suppose that
g,b\in L^{p}\left(\mu\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
and define
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
-projections,

\displaystyle g\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}g_{k}\left(x\right)\text{
and }b\left(x\right)\equiv\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}b_{k}\left(x\right)\ ,\ \ \ \ \text{where}

\displaystyle g_{k}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)=\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)=\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)\ }\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)\ }\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right),

\displaystyle b_{k}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{L},k}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)=\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)=\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}b\left(x\right),

along with their corresponding sequences using capital letters,

\displaystyle G_{k}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}=\left\{\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)},

\displaystyle B_{k}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}=\left\{\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}.

Given
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{0\right\}
, we also define more
sequences using capital letters,

\displaystyle G_{m,L}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\text{ and }B_{L}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)},

\displaystyle G_{m,k}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\text{ and }B_{k}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}=\left\{G_{m,k}\left(x\right)\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\text{ and }B^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\ ,

\displaystyle G_{m,k}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}=\left\{G_{m,L}\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\text{ and }B_{k}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)}\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\ ,

where the superscript
\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}
designates a doubly indexed sequence.
We will often write simply

(8.12)

\displaystyle G_{m}\left(x\right)\text{ in place of }G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left(x\right),

\displaystyle B\left(x\right)\text{ in place of }B^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left(x\right).

Note that the projection in the numerator of (
8.10
) can be written
in several different ways,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\bigcup_{K\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\mathcal{D}\left(K\right)}^{\mu}g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left(K\right)}^{\mu}g=\sum_{K\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\left(\sum_{M\in\mathcal{L}:\ M\subset K}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(M\right)}^{\mu}g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}:\ L^{\prime}\subset L}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g=\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}:\ L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g,

where in the final expression, we are using the tree ordering on
\mathcal{D}\left[T\right]
. From the square function equivalences we then have

\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\bigcup_{K\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\mathcal{D}\left(K\right)}^{\mu}g\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}\approx\left\|\left|\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}\approx\left\|\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}\left(x\right)_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)},

where the sequence to which the norm
\left|\cdot\right|_{\ell^{2}}
applies is understood by context. For example,

\displaystyle\left|\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\text{,}

\displaystyle\text{and }\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}\left(x\right)_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\{\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}\right\}_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(L^{\prime},I\right)\in\mathcal{L}\times\mathcal{D}\\
L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L,I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)\end{subarray}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2},

where by context, the iterated sequence
G_{m,L^{\prime}}\left(x\right)_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}=\left\{\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}\right\}_{L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L}
is
understood to be the sequence
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(L^{\prime},I\right)\in\mathcal{L}\times\mathcal{D}\\
L^{\prime}\preccurlyeq L,I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)\end{subarray}}
, with some ordering of
the countable set of such intervals
I
.

Define

\displaystyle G_{m,L}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{G_{m,K}\left(x\right)\right\}_{K\in\mathcal{L}:\ K\subset L}\text{ and }B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{B_{K}\left(x\right)\right\}_{K\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(L\right)},

\displaystyle G_{m,k}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{G_{m,L}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\ \text{ and }B_{k}^{\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\ ,

where the last line can be interpreted as doubly indexed sequences. Note
that for each
fixed

k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
m\in\mathbb{N},
and
0\leq\ell\leq N
, both collections of functions

\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\text{ and }\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}

have pairwise disjoint supports in both
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathbb{R}
, i.e.
both (
3.20
) and (
3.22
) hold for each collection of
functions. Thus from Corollary
24
, we have for each fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

(8.13)

\displaystyle\left\|\left|G_{m,k}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\lesssim\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\left\|\left|G_{m,L}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\leq M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\left\|\left|B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\left\|\left|\sum_{\ell=0}^{N}B_{L}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\lesssim N^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{N}\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\left\|\left|B_{L}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle N^{p+1}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\max_{0\leq\ell\leq N}\left\|\left|B_{k}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\leq N^{p+1}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\left\|\left|B_{k}^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p},

where
B_{L}^{\left(\ell\right)}\equiv\left\{B_{K}\left(x\right)\right\}_{K\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(L\right)}
.

Note that we cannot apply Corollary
24
to the doubly
indexed sequences

G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}=\left\{\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\bigcup_{K\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\mathcal{D}\left(K\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\text{ and }B_{N}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}=\left\{\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

since we lose the pairwise disjoint property in both
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathbb{R}
. We must work harder to handle this general situation and the
remainder of the proof is devoted to this end. We begin by treating the
function
g
, and then
b
will be treated using similar ideas at the end of
the argument.

Let
\lambda>1
. For each
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)
with
L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)
, define

(8.14)

\displaystyle\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{x\in L^{\prime}:\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}>\lambda M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)\left|B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\},

\displaystyle\text{and }\Omega_{m,N,k}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\bigcup_{L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k+1\right)}\left(T\right)}\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}\ ,

and note that
\Omega_{m,N,k+1}\subset\Omega_{m,N,k}
since
\left|G_{m,L^{\prime\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\leq\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\,
for
L^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)
. Then on
L^{\prime}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}
, we have the pointwise inequality
\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\leq\lambda M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(g,b\right)\left|B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}
, and if we write

(8.15)

\displaystyle G_{m}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}=\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L\in\mathcal{L}\\
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L\in\mathcal{L}\\
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)\end{subarray}}+\left\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L\in\mathcal{L}\\
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}+G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}},

then it follows that

(8.16)

\displaystyle\left\|\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}=\left\|\left|\left\{\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(L^{\prime}\right)}^{\mu}g\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L\in\mathcal{L}\\
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)\end{subarray}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\left\|\left|\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L\in\mathcal{L}\\
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)\end{subarray}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\lesssim\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\left\|\left|B^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}.

To handle the term
G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}}
we must work harder. Now since
\left|B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}
is constant on
each set
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)
when
m>N
, we note that for
m>N
,
L\in\mathcal{L}
and
\,L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)
,

(8.17)

\displaystyle\left|\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}\right|_{\mu}=\left|L^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}\right|_{\mu}\leq\int_{L^{\prime}}\frac{\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}}{\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\left|B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}}d\mu\left(x\right)=\frac{\int_{L^{\prime}}\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)}{\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}E_{L^{\prime}}^{\mu}\left|B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}}\frac{\int_{L^{\prime}}\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)}{\int_{L^{\prime}}\left|B_{L}^{\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)}\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}\leq C_{0}\frac{1}{\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}=\frac{C_{0}}{\lambda^{p}}\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}\ ,

since

\displaystyle\left\|\left|G_{m,L^{\prime}}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\sum_{K\subset L^{\prime}}G_{m,K}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\sum_{K\subset L^{\prime}}\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right)}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\approx\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\bigcup_{G\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L\right)}\mathcal{D}\left(G\right)}^{\mu}g\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p},

by the square function estimates in Theorem
18
. In particular,
for
\lambda>C_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}
we have

(8.18)

\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}\geq\left|L^{\prime}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}\right|_{\mu}=\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}-\left|\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}\right|_{\mu}\geq\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}-\frac{C_{0}}{\lambda^{p}}\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}=\left(1-\frac{C_{0}}{\lambda^{p}}\right)\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}\ ,

independent of
L^{\prime}
and
m
and
N
. The facts that
\left|G_{m,k}^{\left[\infty\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}
is
constant on
L^{\prime}
for
L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(L\right)
and
L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)
, and that

(8.19)

\left|L^{\prime}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L^{\prime}}\right|_{\mu}\approx\left|L^{\prime}\right|_{\mu},

will permit us to replace the sequence
\left\{g_{k}\right\}_{k\geq 1}
of
functions with
m
sequences that are pairwise disjoint in
\mathbb{R}
as
well as in
\mathcal{D}
, and which will in turn permit us to use the
definition of
M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)
on
each of these
m
sequences.

Indeed, for
1\leq\kappa\leq m+1
, define

\displaystyle g^{\left[\kappa\right]}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}}g_{k}\left(x\right)\text{ and }G^{\left[\kappa\right]}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{G_{k}\left(x\right)\right\}_{k\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}}\ ,

\displaystyle\widehat{g^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{m,N,k}}\left(x\right)g_{k}\left(x\right)\text{ and }\widehat{G^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{m,N,k}}\left(x\right)G_{k}\left(x\right)\right\}_{k\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}}\ ,

\displaystyle\widetilde{g^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{m,N,k}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,k+m+1}}\left(x\right)g_{k}\left(x\right)\text{ and }\widetilde{G^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{m,N,k}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,k+m+1}}\left(x\right)G_{k}\left(x\right)\right\}_{k\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}},

so that for each
\kappa
, the sequence of functions
\widetilde{g_{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)
satisfies both (
3.20
) and (
3.22
).

Notation
:
We also make corresponding definitions with the
caret decoration
\widehat{}
and the tilde decoration
\widetilde{}
, analogous to those in (
), but with a superscript
[κ]
. Thus the caret denotes restriction to
\Omega_{m,N,k}
, while tilde denotes restriction to the ‘annular’ set

\Omega_{m,N,k}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,k+m+1}
.

Moreover, recalling our convention (
8.12
) that we often write
G_{m}^{\left[\kappa\right]}\left(x\right)
in place of
G_{m}^{\left[\kappa\right],\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
, etc., we claim that

(8.21)

\left|\widehat{G_{m}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\lesssim\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left(\left|\widetilde{G_{m}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right)\left(x\right),\ \ \ \ \ x\in T,

follows from (
8.18
). To see this, pick a point
x_{0}\in T
. Since
the Haar support of
g
is finite, the sets
\Omega_{m,N,k+m+1}
are
eventually empty, and so there is
k
such that
x_{0}\in\Omega_{m,N,k}\setminus\Omega_{m,N,k+m+1}
. Suppose that
L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)
contains
x_{0}
. Now note
that
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}

is constant on
L
, since if
L^{\ast}=\pi_{\mathcal{L}}L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k-1\right)}\left(T\right)
is the child at level
k-1
that contains
L
, then for
x\in L
,

\left|G_{L^{\ast}}^{\left[\kappa\right]}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}=\left|\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(L^{\ast}\right)}\right|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{I\in\left(L,L^{\ast}\right]}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}g\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\text{ is constant}.

Thus (
8.18
) implies

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right],\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right],\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+\frac{1}{\left|L\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L}\right|_{\mu}}\int_{L\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+\frac{1}{\left|L\right|_{\mu}}\int_{L\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|\widetilde{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|\widetilde{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right)\left(x_{0}\right),

since
\widehat{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)=\widetilde{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)
on
L\setminus\Omega_{m,N,L}
if
s\leq k-1
. We also have
\left|\widehat{G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\left(x\right)=\left|\widetilde{G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\left(x\right)
since
x\notin\Omega_{m,N,k+m+1}
, and thus we conclude

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k\end{subarray}}\left|G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\left(x_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\left(x\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right)+\left|\widehat{G_{k}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\left(x_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\kappa+\left(m+1\right)\mathbb{N}\\
s\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\left|\widetilde{G_{m,s}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right)\left(x_{0}\right)+\left|\widetilde{G_{k}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left(x_{0}\right)\right|_{\ell^{2}}+M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left|\widetilde{G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right)\left(x_{0}\right),

which is (
8.21
).

Altogether then, combining the boundedness of
M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}
on
L^{p}\left(\mu\right)
with Lemma
23
, (
8.16
) and (
8.13
), we have for each
\kappa
and
m
,

\displaystyle\left\|g^{\left[\kappa\right]}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\approx\left\|\left|\left\{G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}\right\}_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\left\|\left|B^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}+\left\|\left(\left|\left\{M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\widetilde{G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right\}_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p},

where, recalling that
b_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}
is
defined in the above
Notation
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\left|\left\{M_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}y}}\widetilde{G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right\}_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\left\{\widetilde{G_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right\}_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\approx\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\left\|\widetilde{g_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\leq\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\left\|g_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right]}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\left\|b_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}=M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|b_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}b_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right),\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }p>1\text{,}

so that altogether,

(8.22)

\left\|g^{\left[\kappa\right]}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\lesssim\lambda^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\left\|\left|B^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}+M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}b_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right).

At this point we write
B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}
as
a sum of
N+1
martingale difference sequences
B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}\left(x\right)
,
0\leq s\leq N
, i.e.

(8.23)

\displaystyle B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)=\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}=\left\{\sum_{s=0}^{N}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(s\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}=\sum_{s=0}^{N}B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{where }B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}\left(x\right)\equiv\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(s\right)}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right)\right\}_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}.

Note that
B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}
is a martingale
difference sequence for the
L^{p}\left(\mu\right)
function

\mathsf{P}b\left(x\right)\equiv\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{L\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(T\right)}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(L\right)}^{\mu}b\left(x\right),

and hence by the square function estimates in Theorem
18
, we
have for each
s
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}b_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)\approx\left\|\left|\left\{\widehat{B_{m,k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}}\right\}_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}=\left\|\left|\left\{B_{k}^{\left(s\right)}\right\}_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p},

and hence that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{g^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}b_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left[N\right]}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}\sum_{s=0}^{N}B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}N^{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right).

Altogether,

\displaystyle\left\|\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa=1}^{m+1}\left\|\widehat{g^{\left[\kappa\right]}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\lesssim M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}N^{p}\sum_{\kappa=1}^{m+1}\sup_{0\leq s\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{k\in\kappa+m\mathbb{N}}B_{k}^{\left[\kappa\right],\left(s\right)}\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle mN^{p}M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)^{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq N}\left\|\left|\left\{B_{k}^{\left(s\right)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}^{p}\ .

Using (
8.16
) and the previous line,

\displaystyle\left\|\left|G\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}+\left\|\left|G_{m}^{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)\left\|\left|B^{\left[N\right]}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}+mNM_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)\sup_{0\leq s\leq N}\left\|\left|\left\{B_{k}^{\left(s\right)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle mNM_{\mathcal{L}}^{\left(m,N\right)}\left(g,b\right)\sup_{0\leq s\leq N}\left\|\left|\left\{B_{k}^{\left(s\right)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\right|_{\ell^{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}\ ,

which completes the proof of inequality (
8.9
) in the conclusion of
the Corona Martingale Comparison Principle.

8.4.
L^{p}
-Stopping Child Lemma

We begin by defining the iteration of general stopping times, which we
remind the reader are simply subsets of the dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
.

Definition 36
.

Suppose
\mathcal{Q\subset D}
, and for each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, let
\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)
with
Q\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]
, which can be thought of
as a family
\left\{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}

of stopping times indexed by
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
. Then we define the
composition
\mathcal{Q\circ A}
to be
\mathcal{Q\circ A}\equiv\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]
, which can also be
written simply as
\mathcal{A}
when the additional structure arising from
\mathcal{Q}
is unimportant.

Recall that the
p
-energy defined in (
6.2
) by,

\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(I,\omega\right)\equiv\left(\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I}\left|x-\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I}zd\omega\left(z\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\approx\left(\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I}\left(\sum_{J\subset I}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

For
\Lambda\subset\mathcal{D}\left[I\right]
let

(8.24)

\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(\Lambda;\omega\right)\equiv\sqrt[p]{\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\int_{I}\left(\sum_{J\in\Lambda}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\left(x\right)}\approx\left\|\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\sum_{J\in\Lambda}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}=\left\|\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\omega}Z\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)},

which generalizes the
p
-energy
\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(I,\omega\right)

defined for an interval
I
- indeed, one immediately checks that
\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(I,\omega\right)=\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(\mathcal{D}\left[I\right];\omega\right)
.

We will state our stopping child lemma in the context of an iterated
stopping time
\mathcal{Q\circ A}
where only
\mathcal{A}
is assumed to
have the structure arising from Lemma
33
. In fact, we will only
apply this lemma later on to the special case
\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}=\mathcal{S}^{\left(n-1\right)}\circ\mathcal{A}_{n}
with
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{S}^{\left(n-1\right)}
and
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{n}
, so we
will state our stopping child lemma only in this case, but observe that the
estimate for the off-diagonal terms here involves no structure from
\mathcal{Q}
, unlike the diagonal terms treated later on, which rely
crucially on the structure of
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{S}^{\left(n-1\right)}
, and which will be treated using the lemmas from the previous subsection.
Here are the details.

We now recursively define the sequence of stopping times
\mathfrak{Q}\equiv\left\{\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
that we
will consider in the remainder of the proof. Set
\mathcal{S}^{\left(0\right)}\equiv\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\left(1\right)}\equiv\mathcal{S}^{\left(0\right)}\mathcal{\circ A}_{1}=\mathcal{F\circ A}_{1}

where
\mathcal{A}_{1}
is constructed using Lemma
33
for a dyadic
tree with the parameter
\Gamma>1
fixed, but close to
1
, and where
\nu=\nu_{\Lambda_{g_{A_{0}}}^{\omega}}
for
A_{0}\in\mathcal{S}^{\left(0\right)}
, and
g_{A_{0}}=\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[A_{0}\right]}g
. See Definition
36
for
\mathcal{F\circ A}_{1}
. Then set
\mathcal{S}^{\left(2\right)}\equiv\mathcal{S}^{\left(1\right)}\mathcal{\circ A}_{2}
where
\mathcal{A}_{2}
is now constructed using Lemma
33
relative to the stopping times
\mathcal{S}^{\left(1\right)}

instead of
\mathcal{S}^{\left(0\right)}=\mathcal{F}
, and with
\nu=\nu_{\Lambda_{g_{A_{1}}}^{\omega}}
for
A_{1}\in\mathcal{A}_{1}\left[F\right]
, and
g_{A_{1}}=\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{D}\left[A_{1}\right]}g
.
Continue by defining recursively,

\mathcal{S}^{\left(n+1\right)}\equiv\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}\mathcal{\circ A}_{n+1},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }n\geq 1.

Note that all of the stopping times
\mathcal{A}_{k}
for
k\geq 1
are
constructed with the same fixed parameter
\Gamma>1
in Lemma
33
,
but with smaller and smaller collections
\Lambda_{g_{A_{n}}}^{\omega}
of

\mathcal{D}
as
n
increases.

We define the separated stopping form

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],A}\left(f,g\right),

where

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],A}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[S\right]}\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\varphi_{J}^{F,S},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

is the local separated form - called ‘separated’ because there is a child
S

separating the intervals
J
from the intervals
I
arising in the sum for
\varphi_{J}^{F,S}
.

Finally for any
sequence

\Lambda_{\mathcal{Q}}=\left\{\Lambda_{Q}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}
of subsets
\Lambda_{Q}\subset\mathcal{D}\left[Q\right]
for
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, and
\delta>0
, we define

(8.25)

\displaystyle\left|f\right|_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda_{\mathcal{Q}}}\left(x\right)\equiv\sqrt{\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}2^{-\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(A,Q\right)\delta}\sum_{S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood},\tau}\left(S\right)}\alpha_{A}\left(S\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(K,\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus S}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(K\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{S;K}^{\omega,\Lambda_{Q}}\right|Z\left(x\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ where }\mathsf{P}_{S;K}^{\omega,\Lambda_{Q}}h\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\Lambda_{Q}:\ J\subset_{\tau}\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}\left[S\right]\\
J\subset K\end{subarray}}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}h\text{ and }\left|\mathsf{P}_{S;K}^{\omega,\Lambda_{Q}}\right|h\equiv\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\Lambda_{Q}:\ J\subset_{\tau}\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}\left[S\right]\\
J\subset K\end{subarray}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}h\right|^{2}}.

Certain special cases of this rather complicated expression can be thought
of as substituting for the role of Lacey’s size condition, but constrained
to live in the world of the measure
\omega
.

In our application of the
L^{p}
-Stopping Child Lemma, the main hypothesis (
8.28
) below will follow from iterating the negation of the
first line in (
8.5
) of the dual tree decomposition.

Lemma 37

(Quadratic
L^{p}
-Stopping Child Lemma)
.

Let
1<p<\infty
, and
f\in L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)
,
g\in L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\omega\right)
have their Haar supports in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{c}hild}}
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a
collection of
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
stopping times satisfying a
\sigma
-Carleson
condition. Let
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{S}^{\left(n-1\right)}
so that
\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)

is a set of
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
stopping times with top
F
, and note that
\mathcal{Q}=\bigcup_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]
. Set
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}
so that for each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, the collection
\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)
is a set of
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
stopping times with top
interval
Q
. For
A\in\mathcal{A}
and
S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)
, set

(8.26)

\alpha_{A}\left(S\right)\equiv\sup_{I\in\left(\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}\left[S\right],A\right]\cap\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}\left|E_{I}^{\sigma}f\right|,

where
\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}\left[S\right]
is the smallest interval in
the Haar support
\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}
of
f
that contains
S
. Finally,
we suppose there is
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
such that for all

\left(F,Q,A,S,K\right)\in\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{Q}\left[F\right]\times\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]\times\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)\times\mathcal{W}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{t}rip}}\left(S\right)\text{ and }m\geq 1,

we have the equivalence,

(8.27)

\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)\cap\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left[K\right]\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left[\cup\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left(N+1\right)}\left(S\right)\cap K\right]\right\}\cap\Lambda_{g}^{\omega};\omega\right)\approx\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)\cap\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left[K\right]\cap\Lambda_{g}^{\omega};\omega\right),

and also the geometric decay bound (which is trivial when
m=1
),

(8.28)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)\cap\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left[\bigcup_{B\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(S\right)}B\cap K\right]\right\}\cap\Lambda_{g}^{\omega};\omega\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C2^{-m\delta}\mathsf{E}_{p}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)\cap\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left[K\right]\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\left[\cup\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left(N+1\right)}\left(S\right)\cap K\right]\right\}\cap\Lambda_{g}^{\omega};\omega\right),

where
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left(k\right)}\left(S\right)
is
defined in Notation
26
(and note that
\delta
in(
8.28
) is different than the
\delta
appearing in (
3.8
)).

Then we have the following nonlinear bound for all
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}\supset\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{S}^{\left(n-1\right)}\supset\mathcal{F}
,

(8.29)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(1+2^{N\delta}\right)\frac{N^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\left\|\left|f\right|_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{A}}^{\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(A\right)\cap\Lambda_{g_{Q}}^{\omega}\right\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ 1<p<\infty.

Note that in the definition of
\left|f\right|_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{A}}^{\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(A\right)\cap\Lambda_{g_{Q}}^{\omega}\right\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}}}
in (
8.25
), the restrictions
J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(S\right)
and
J\subset K\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood},\tau}\left(S\right)
on the intervals
J
arising in the absolute
projection
\left|\mathsf{P}_{S;K}^{\omega,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(A\right)\cap\Lambda_{g_{Q}}^{\omega}}\right|
, imply that
K
satisfies

(8.30)

K\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood},\tau}^{\mathcal{A},\left[N\right]}\left(S\right)\equiv\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left[N\right]}\left(S\right)\cap\mathcal{W}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood},\tau}\left(S\right).

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{A}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\omega}g\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,B\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]\\
B\subsetneqq A\end{subarray}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(B\right)}^{\omega}g\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{B\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m\right)}\left(A\right)}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(B\right)}^{\omega}g\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m\right)}\left(A\right)}^{\omega}g\right),

where for each
m\geq 1
,
F\in\mathcal{F}
and
A\in\mathcal{A}\left[F\right]
, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m\right)}\left(A\right)}^{\omega}g\right)=\sum_{S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(S\right)}^{\omega}g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(I,J\right)\in\left(S,A\right]\times\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[S\right]\right\}\\
J\subset_{\tau}I\end{subarray}}\left(E_{I_{J}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus S}\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(S\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(S\right)}\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\varphi_{J}^{\mathcal{A},F,S},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(S\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\ ,

where

\varphi_{J}^{\mathcal{A},F,S}\equiv\sum_{I\in\left(S,A\right]:\ J\subset_{\tau}\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}\left[S\right]}\left(E_{I_{J}}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus S},\ \ \ \ \ A=\pi_{\mathcal{A}}S\ ,

and where
\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}\left[S\right]
is the smallest interval
in the Haar support
\Lambda_{f}^{\sigma}
of
f
that strictly contains
S
. We rename,

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[F\right],A,m}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{F},F}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}\left(A\right)}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right]}^{\left(m\right)}\left(A\right)}^{\omega}g\right),

and write,

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],A}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\left(Q\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[S\right]}\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\varphi_{J}^{\mathcal{A},F,S},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],A,m}\left(f,g\right)\ ,

where

\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],A,m}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\sum_{S\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(A\right)}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\left(m-1\right)}\left(S\right)}\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\varphi_{J}^{\mathcal{A},F,S},\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\ .

Recalling our convention regarding iterated sums, we define

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A},m}\left(f,g\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{Q}\circ\mathcal{A},m}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}\mathop{\mathrm{s}ep}}^{\mathcal{A}\left[Q\right],A,m}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=
