\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{\left(F,Q_{n},S,K\right)\in\Omega^{4}}\Psi_{Q_{n}}^{p}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}}\left(Q_{n}\right)}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\sigma\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle N^{\frac{2-p}{2}n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{Q_{0}\in\mathcal{S}^{\left(0\right)}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{S}^{\left(0\right)}}\left(Q_{0}\right)}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\sigma\left(x\right)=N^{\frac{2-p}{2}n}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\sigma\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle N^{\frac{2-p}{2}n}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{F}\left|f\right|d\sigma\right)^{p}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}+\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}}\int_{F^{\prime}}\left|f\right|d\sigma\right)^{p}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}\right\}\lesssim N^{\frac{2-p}{2}n}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},

where the last line follows from (
6.3
), the pointwise inequality,

\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\sigma}f\left(x\right)\right|\lesssim\sup_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left(E_{I}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)+\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left(E_{F^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\mathbf{1}_{F^{\prime}}\left(x\right)\lesssim\left(E_{F}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\mathbf{1}_{F}\left(x\right)+\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left(E_{F^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left|f\right|\right)\mathbf{1}_{F^{\prime}}\left(x\right),

the quasiorthogonality inequality (3) in the subsection on Carleson measures
above, and the
\sigma
-Carleson property of
\mathcal{F}
. This completes
the proof of the case
1<p\leq 2
with the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\left|f\right|_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{S}^{\left(n-1\right)},\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}}^{\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}}^{\left(s\right)}\left(A\right)\cap\Lambda_{g_{A}}^{\omega}\right\}_{A\in\mathcal{S}^{\left(n\right)}}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\left(C_{p}\theta^{\frac{p}{2}}\right)^{\frac{n}{p}}N^{n\frac{2-p}{2p}}\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F};p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle C_{p}^{\frac{n}{p}}\theta^{\frac{n}{2}}\left(\frac{C}{\theta}\right)^{\frac{n}{p}-\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F};p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}=\left(\left(C_{p}C^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\right)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{p}}\theta\right)^{\frac{n}{p^{\prime}}}\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F};p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}.

Now Lemma
27
yields,

\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F};p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right),

which completes the proof of Lemma
38
.

This finishes our control of the stopping form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}\left(f,g\right)
in (
8.1
) for
1<p<4
. The dual stopping
form requires
1<p^{\prime}<4
.

Now we turn to the final ingredient in the proof Theorems
5

and
7
, which is the control of the extended energy characteristic
by the norm characteristic.

9.
Control of extended energy

Fix
p\in\left(\frac{4}{3},2\right)\cup\left(2,4\right)
and consider
the two ‘dual’ intertwining forms arising in the Intertwining Proposition,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{i}nter}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{u}pper}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{i}nter}}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{G\in\mathcal{G}}\ \sum_{J:\ J\supsetneqq G}\ \left\langle H_{\omega}\left(\mathbf{1}_{J_{G}}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}}\left(G\right)}^{\sigma}f\right\rangle_{\sigma}\ ,

With

\displaystyle g_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\text{ },

\displaystyle f_{F}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f=\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\left\langle f,h_{I}\right\rangle h_{I}=\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbb{E}_{\theta I_{F}}f\ ,

the proof of the Intertwining Proposition shows that
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{i}nter}}\left(f,g\right)
satisfies

(9.1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{i}nter}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\equiv\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}f_{F},\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}f_{F}\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}+\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\equiv\mathsf{I}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(f,g\right)\ ,

where the form
\mathsf{I}\left(f,g\right)
is controlled in (
6.10
)
above by local quadratric testing,

\left|\mathsf{I}\left(f,g\right)\right|=\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F}f_{F}\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{H,p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}.

Let
\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
denote the best constant in the bilinear inequality
for
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(f,g\right)
, i.e.

(9.2)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(f,g\right)\right|=\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)},

and similarly define the ‘dual’ characteristic
\mathfrak{U}_{H,p^{\prime},\mathop{\mathrm{u}pper}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)

as the bound for the bilinear form
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{u}pper}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(f,g\right)
.

Lemma 39
.

For
1<p<\infty
we have

\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\approx\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\text{ and }\mathfrak{U}_{H,p^{\prime},\mathop{\mathrm{u}pper}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\approx\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt},\ast}\left(\omega,\sigma\right).

Proof.

Upon considering positive and negative parts of a function, we may assume
without loss of generality that both
f\geq 0
and
g\geq 0
when bounding the Hilbert transform bilinear form
\left\langle H_{\sigma}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}
at the outset of the proof.

First note by duality that the bilinear inequality

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(f,g\right)\right|=\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\langle\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right),g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

is equivalent to the norm inequality

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\right)^{p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p}\ .

Now we exploit the fact that
\left\langle Z,h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\geq 0
, our assumption that
f\geq 0
, and the fact that the
support of the projection
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}^{\omega}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\omega}
is
separated
from the support of the
function
\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}
. By the Monotonicity Lemma,

(9.3)

\left\langle H_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\right),h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\approx\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(J,\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\left\langle Z,h_{J}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\ ,

since

\displaystyle f_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{N}\left(E_{F_{m+1}}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F_{m+1}\setminus F_{m}}=\left(E_{F}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F}+\sum_{m=0}^{N}\left(E_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(E_{F}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{F}+\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset F^{\prime}}\left(E_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\setminus F^{\prime}}\ \geq 0.

We conclude that
\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
is comparable to the best constant, which
we continue to denote by
\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
, in the norm inequality,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(J,\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset F^{\prime}}\left(E_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\setminus F^{\prime}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\omega\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{p}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }f\geq 0.

By the square function theorem,
\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}
is thus comparable to the best constant in the
inequality,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(J,\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset F^{\prime}}\left(E_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\setminus F^{\prime}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\left(\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{p}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }f\geq 0,

equivalently,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{r}-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(W,\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}:\ F\subset F^{\prime}}\left(E_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}}^{\sigma}f\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime}\setminus F^{\prime}}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(W\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|Z\left(x\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\left(\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{p}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }f\geq 0,

where
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{r}-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)
consists of
the maximal
\mathbf{r}
-deeply embedded subcubes of
F
, and

\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|Z\left(x\right)\equiv\left(\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}Z\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We can rewrite the sum
\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{W\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{r}-\mathop{\mathrm{d}eep}}\left(F\right)}
as
\sum_{W\in\mathbb{W}}
due
to the fact that
W
determines
F
uniquely. If we write
\mathsf{P}_{W}^{\omega}=\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}
for
W\in\mathbb{W}
, then we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{W\in\mathbb{W}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(W,\mathbf{1}_{F^{c}}f_{F}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(W\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|^{2}Z\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{p}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }f\geq 0.

For
F\in\mathcal{F}
, where
\mathcal{F}
is the collection of stopping
times associated with a function
f
, define
\Phi_{F}f
by

\Phi_{F}f\equiv\mathbb{E}_{F}^{\sigma}f+f_{F}=\mathbb{E}_{F}^{\sigma}f+\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\mathbf{1}_{I_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f.

Then the ‘plugged’ norm

\int\left(\sum_{W\in\mathbb{W}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(W,\mathbf{1}_{F}\Phi_{F}f\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(W\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|^{2}Z\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega,

can be controlled using the fact that the collection
\mathbb{W}
is
\sigma
-Carleson. Thus altogether we obtain that
\mathfrak{U}_{H,p,\mathop{\mathrm{l}ower}}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{h}ole}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
is comparable to
the best constant
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
in the extended energy inequality,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{W\in\mathbb{W}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(W,\Phi_{F}f\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(W\right)}\right)^{2}\left|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)\cap\mathcal{D}\left[W\right]}^{\omega}\right|^{2}Z\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}d\omega\lesssim\left(\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\ell^{2},\mathop{\mathrm{e}xt}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{p}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }f\geq 0.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

Lemma
39
, together with the discussion at the beginning of this
section, and the earlier estimates on the other forms in this paper,
finishes the proofs of Theorems
5
,
7
,
8
and
9
.

10.
Concluding remarks on the stopping form

The methods we used above for bounding the stopping form, actually yield the
following weaker form of (
8.50
), with a smaller
\ell^{q}
norm
inside,

(10.1)

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\left\{\alpha_{A}\left(S\right)\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}\left(K,\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus S}\sigma\right)}{\ell\left(K\right)}\right)\mathsf{P}_{S;K}^{\omega,\Lambda_{g_{A}}}Z\left(x\right)\ \mathbf{1}_{K}\left(x\right)\right\}_{\left(F,A,S,K\right)\in\Omega^{4}}\right|_{\ell^{q}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\omega\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(C_{p}\theta\right)^{n\left(2-\frac{p}{q}\right)}\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F};p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{p}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\sigma\right)}^{p}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }2\leq q\leq p<2q.

This follows easily upon applying Hölder’s inequality with exponent
\frac{p}{q}
in (
8.51
), and helps shed light on why our method fails
to prove the
L^{p}
conjectures for all
1<p<\infty
. Indeed, experience
in this paper and in
[
SaWi
]
, suggests that we must manipulate the left
hand side of (
8.50
) or (
10.1
) in the world of the measure
\omega
, to obtain some gain in
\theta
before applying a characteristic,
such as
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{F};p}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
, to
transfer analysis to the world of the measure
\sigma
. But this prevents
us from exploiting the ‘orthogonality’ in the function
f
that is hidden in
the coefficents
\alpha_{A}\left(S\right)
, while obtaining a gain in
\theta
, something that is avoided when
p=2
using the Orthogonality
Argument of Lacey in
[
Lac
]
, see the subsection on heuristics above.

As a consequence, we are forced to work in the world of the measure
\omega

with ‘one hand tied behind our back’, and perform sums in the factors
\Psi_{A}
which produce large negative powers of
\frac{1}{\theta}
, which
ultimately accounts for the restriction to
\frac{4}{3}<p<4
.
